it is with this doc. as tis with the doc. that treat of things that are in another O that are invisible things things that & theref. are things that are hard to be believed. what is wrought in the soul in Conver saving Conversion is as much unseen to a natural man as Heaven is. & theref as it is with the doc. of another O men men are very ready to suspect all that is said about it & to [--] Look upon it as a Kind of dream & to be raising many objections against it suspect [^001-note2] so me unexperienced men are ready to Look on what is said About Conversion is only the [ ] [ ] of diss either from Lying & dissimulation or from their fancy fancy & vain Imagination & there are many difficulties & objections that they raise against & say as nicodemus did on frequent occasions as Nicodemus did how Can these things be I shall theref. now Endeavour to offer some plain Reasons to natural men tending to their Conviction of the Reality of such a thing thing as Conversion.

There are but three ways that the truth of any thing can be made manifest . viz. Reason SS. & Experience. I would theref shew how Each of these shews that there is such a thing as Conversion

_________________

I. Reason shews that there is such a thing as Conversion . This may appear by b in that the following particulars are manifest by to Reason.

_______________________________________

The Consideration of the following things will make this manifest.

______________________

Here I would shew in particular how several things are manifest which being put together make it manifest that there is such a thing as Conversion or such a Change Change as as as we have commonly been taught to understand by the word [^001-note3] Conversion.

1. mans happiness consists in his union with his Creatour Reason teaches this man is for tis manifest that man was not made for the same Kind no higher happiness than the brute Crea. Consisting onl in the Enjoymts of External things or sensitive objects. because he is made with very diverse faculties so exceedingl diverse of man had no higher faculties than his External [ ] senses than we might Reasonabl as tis with the Brutes than we might with [later xo] some Reason supposed that he was made for no [later add.] higher happiness than what Consists in in the objects of those senses but seeing it is not but he has those faculties that are so much superiour by which is capable of the Knowl. of his creatour Creatour of all things which the beasts Gives Give not the Least signs of any th apprehension of we must suppose that it must be some thing of a higher Kind that mans happiness Con sists in & this can be in no other than the by union with his Creatour . for how unrea sonable would it be to suppose that a Creature that is capable of Knowing his Creatour should be happy in a state of alienation from & the being his Creatour or while he & that being that made him gave him being & [^001-note4] upholds him every moment & that he depends upon every way . are at odds one with another & as it & in a state of distance opposition & war one with another.

### 560. Eph. 4:29

### 560.Eph. 4'29(7_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

______________________________________________________

Eph. 4. 29. Let no Corrupt Communication proceed out of your mouth but that which is good to the use of Edifying that it may minister Grace unto the Hearers.

_________________

The words contain an Exh. of the Apostle to the Xtian Ephesians Respecting their speech or discourse one with another. wherein which consists of two parts . 1. there is something dehorted from viz. corrup 1. a declaration wherein an Let no Corruptive communication proceed out of your mouth wh in which part may be observed two things 1 The thing dehorted from or advised against viz viz Corrupt Communication. the word in the original that is translated Corrupt properly signifies Rotten as the flesh of a noisom sore or as the flesh of a rotten stinking carcass Intimating that such Kind of Communication as often proceeds out of the mouths of wicked men is foul & filthy more Loathsom than the Corruption of a stinking wound or than the Rotten flesh of [-] Carrion. which men would abhor to have in their mouths much more should they abhor to have such Corrupt filthy talk in their mouths as wicked men sometimes have 2 we may Observe the manner in which [^003-note1] the Apostle advises against it . dont Let it proceed out of your minds mouth i.e. utterly avoid it Let never be guilty of Let not your Conscience hav ever have it to accuse you of that ever such d any such words come forth out of your mouth. The mouth is a part of our bodies in which we abhor to have that which is outwardly corrupt . as Corrupt & stinking flesh & but we should we Abhor to have that it enter into our mouths but much more should we abhor to have that which is morally corrupt & filthy proceed out of our mouths for not that which going into the mouth but that which cometh out of the mouth defiles the man Math 15. 11.

______________________________________________________

2 The other part of the advice of the that the Apostle here gives is an exh. wherein something is Exhorted to. viz wherein may be observed two things . 1. The things advised which is vertuous & profitable discourse . there are two phrases use that is is good that which is Good to the use of Edifying i. e that which good in it self & tends to p do [-] Good to [^003-note2] others tends t such discourse whereby you promote the good of Each others souls.

______________________________________________________

2. The End good End which the apo & Great Benefit wit of th such discourse which the with which the Apostle Enforces this advice That it may minister Grace to the hearers such discourse tends to the best & most Excellent benefit of those that hear[-] it it tends to minister Grace to them which is the most Excellent Benefit than any can Recieve & is a thousand times better than if it ministered silver & Gold or Earthly pleasures or temporal Health or any other good whatsoever.

______________________________________________________

doc. Professing Xtians when they meet together should avoid all corrupt discourse one with another & should practice that whereby they may promote the good of Each others souls.

______________________________________________________

I. They should avoid all corrupt Communication or discourse. as particularly. 1. They should avoid all Profane Conversation all speaking lightly of those things that are of a sacred and divine nature. this is what many persons Especially young persons are prop prone to b in a Corrupt time the being of a serious & [^003-note3] solemn sp. & shewing a Great Regard to things of Relig. is out of Credit among young People and those that appear to be of such a sp. they are ready to call precise very apt to call precise & to Charge with a being foolishly scrupulous . & very often with being hypocrites & proud of their Religion so that and of having a mind to be appear better than others . & on the Contrary they are ready to look upon it something Credible to be & what will recommnd em to their Compani ons to speak of the things of Rel to s make a Jest of things of Relig. or at Least to speak lightly of such thing with a Jesting air

_________

such Conversation is very corrupt & very provoking to G. & ought is more fit for Heathens than for Xtians[-] tis speak to speak Lightly of things of Relig. is to [-] speak lightly of G. & Contemn him & is so looked upon of G.

______________________________________________________

2. They should avoid all unclean or Lascivious Communication . Tho many There are many Persons Especially young persons that are full of such Conversation as this . they are foul m mouthed persons properly so and do very mu seem to delight in that sort unclean Kind songs of & [^003-note4]

& in talking with a bold air about those of those things that are can again modesty for- & seem to Look upon as their honour that they dare break over Rules of modesty in their talk as if it was a Great attainmt a very Commendable Courage [-] to shew that they bent afraid to speak those things that modest Persons Choose to avoid mentioning

___________________

with many young People he is the best man that shews most of that Kind of wit & Courage that appears in unclean Lascivious talk Jesting their mirth & diversion wh consists very much in such Kind of mirth.

_____________________

[--] This exceeding unbecoming Xtian such Persons how much soever they may value thems. on their [-] Impudence and unclean wit their appear more like beasts brutes than Xtiansty true Xtianity is abhorrent to any such thing as Light is to darkness & [ ] & the Exercise of Ho Xtian holiness will make men more to abhor to take such unclean words & su talk into their mouths than wou they would abhor to take take a piece of a rott dead rotten carcass full of worms into their mouths. The apostle advises that such unclean communication be not so much as once named among Xtians. Eph. 5. 3. But fornica. & all uncleanness & Covetousness Let it not be once named amongst you as becometh saints neither fil- [^003-note5] thiness nor foolish talking nor Jesting which are not Convenient. It Requires Xtians whose bodies are the temples of G. & who are Persons devoted to the holy Jesus to be pure in heart & mouth . they should follow after whatsoever things are pure Philip 4. 8. Xtians are the Children of the light & of the day & therefore should walk as Children of the Light Rom 13. 13. Let us walk honestly as in the day not in Chambering & wantonness . Christians are advised by the apostle in Col. 3. 5. 8. to put away all filthy communication out of their mouths & advises in Colos. 4. 6. that their speech should be alwaies with Grace seasoned with salt. salt is [-] [-] used to preserve meats from putrefying & becoming rotten carrion as otherwise they would do so our words should be Kept from uncleanness which makes em like the odious corruption filthy noisom Corruption.

______________________________________________________

3. Another sort of Corrupt discourse that Xtians should avoid when they meet together is talking against others. Great part of the Conversation of young People when they get together consists in in this in making thems. diversion with others faults th faults & blemishes. ridicul very much of the mirth & Laughter that young [^003-note6] People have together in consists in making a Jest of talking & Laughing at these & those things that others that are absent have said & done . They seem to take a pride to speak of these & th others behind their backs with an air of Contempt & make sport of the those things in them that they Reckon shew how weak & foolish they are . they spend a Great deal of time in talking against other young People & th also in ridiculing ol those old People & so they entertain one another & pass away the time that they spend together . which --- which is very unbecoming Professing Xtians a Great many stories are told the against others & it is well if many false & slanderous Reports are not told among others & at Least things are set out beyond what the truth to make others appear the more Ridiculous & m to make thems. the more sport for thems.

__________________

which is very unbecoming professing Xtians when it is Enquired in the 15. who shall abide ---- --------------- [-] it is answered he that backbiteth not with his tongue & it is said in the 101 Ps. 5. whosoever privily slandereth his neighb. will I cut cut off The apostle advises in the 4 of Eph. 31. v. that all Evilspeakings [^003-note7] be put away from among Xtians & so the Apostle 1 Pet. 7. 2. 2. 1. -- The Apostle James does particularly warn Xtians against this Jam. 4. 11. speak not Evil one of another Brethren . He that speaketh evil of his Brother ---------------

______________________________________________________

II. Xtians when they meet are together should use such discourse as may be profitable & thems. to su discourse whereby they may promote the Good of Each others souls. 1. They should use thems. to such discourse as is vertuous innocent & vertuous agreable to the Rule of Gods words watching diligently over their mou setting a watch upon the door of their lips. Keeping their mouth as with cord & bridle Ps. 39. 1. I said I will take heed to my ways I will Keep my mouth with a bridle using their tongues for the Glory of G. & as becomes Reasonable Creatures & Xtians avoiding all appearance of profaneness . in all foolish talking & Jesting all backbiting. being blameless & harmless as the sons of G. & h pure & honourable in their Conversation

________________________________

2 They should accustom thems. to such Conversa. as it may tend that may tend to each others Instruction & Growth in Xtian Knowl. in therefore * 4. I sh another sort of discourse that may be [^003-note8] called corrupt & that professing Xtians should avoid is unseas that which is unseasonable or unsui table for the Time as when Persons when they are together on the sabbath will talk of Oly & indifferent matters use use diverting Kind of discourse or talk of Θly & indiff. matters as on other days . that talk that it at another time may be innocent may become very corrupt by the unseasonableness of it so when so when Persons will as it were mix Relig. & vain & diverting discourse together as when Persons come together under pretence of Keeping a Re holding a Relig. meeting for reading & praying & such Relig. exercises & will Immediately as soon as the Ex Relig. Exercise is over fall into Loose diverting to Jesting & Laughing or go into vain diverting sort of talk Je manifesting that they have bu attended Relig. meerly out of Custom that they have done it as a task & are Glad it is over & now it is over are willing to get as far from it as ever they can . this is but to make a mock of duties of Relig. & when they are attended after this manner they had better be Let [^003-note9] alone . when things are thus disagreably mixed together it Renders both their Relig. Exercise & their discourse too abominable to G. This is something like the Jews bringing their merchandizes their sheep & oxen & doves & Θly concerns into the Temple at which X expressed so much Indignation.

______________________________________________________

Knowl. of the things of G. they should discourse of the doc. & principles of Relig. enquiring one of another & entring into such discourse as tends to the Enlightning [-] one of another They d should discourse together of the word of G. of things that are Contained in the Holy SS. tending to Engage one another in Reading & searching the SS. & helping each other to understand the meaning of it They should be discoursing together of things that they hear in the preaching of the word. which would be an Excellent means of fastening things that they hear in their memory & engaging their attention more in hearing for the time to come & causing that the preaching of the word should take the Greater Impression on their minds [^003-note10] & would prevent their hearing all only in a Customary slighty manner Loosing all immediately as soon as they come out of the meeting house. they should use thems. to discourse together of the works of G. and of in Creation & Provid. & above all in the w. of Redemption Ps. 105. 2. talk ye of all his wondrous works & Ps. 145. 10. 11. 12. ------

_____________________________________________

------------- This that is here spoken of as the practice of the saints is an Excellent practice most becoming the saints .

______________________________________________________

3. They should accustom thems. when together to su talk together of such things as tend to the awakening one another & rousing each others souls out a careless dull & dead frame & begetting in each other a deep sense of the Infinite Importance of the things of Relig. & should be much in speaking of the vanity of the things of the Θ the shortness & uncertainty of Life [-] & how Great a thing it is to die & should be talking of the Great things of together of the Great things of another Θ of speaking of the day of Judgmt & the Great things that will come to pass time speaking of Heaven & hell & talking much of Eternity. [^003-note11]

______________________________

4. They should be much in talking together of such things as tend to each others warning. speaking of the Evil & danger of such & such sinfull practices the folly of ways the corruption of the Heart of men & its Readiness to fall in with tempta. & the Great need we stand in of divine help. & th should particularly be speaking of the Evil of such Kind of sins as persons of their Age & their Circumstances are Especially ready to fall into.

______________________________________________________

5 4. They should be much in fall talking of such things as te have a tendency to enkindle & warm holy affections in Each others souls . as partic so they should talk much when together of Jesus X & his Excellency & the wonderf. Grace of G. in giving him to die for sinners they should be speaking much of the dying Love of J. X They should be speaking much of the Great blessedness Benefits & blessings of the Gospel that are bestowed on the saints the Glorious happiness of the state of such as have truly an Interest in X they should be much in speaking of the saints happiness & Glory in Heaven where they will be perfectly Holy & happy [^003-note12] in the full enjoymt of G. & X & in perfect Love & friendship one with another forever & Ever They should be much in speaking of things that convey vital vit piety & the influence that the Holy sp. of G. has upon the souls of true saints & of the Exercises of the Graces of the Holy sp.

______________________________________________________

& their temptat 6 5. ---- They should be much in talking of such things as tend to Ins direct & stir up each other to their duty should be talking of their duty it should be mat[t]er of discourse among em what is their duty in such & such Cases & of the difficulties & temptations that are Commonly to be met with in the way of the duty. & & of the need of care & of divine help. in duty . & the they should be frequent in such discourse so that they may inure thems. to it & take heed that they dont Keep their hand in if I may so speak & that they dont [-] that so it [-] may alwaies be easy & natural to em to fall into such discourse. for the Less they use thems. to it the more difficulties will increase in the way of entring into it & the more they accustom thems. to it the more easy pleasant will it be. Application. [^003-note13] The Use that I would make of this doc. is To Exhort those here present especially young Persons for whose sake this meeting was principally intended. to comply with the duty recommended in the doc. avoid Let me now this day war who now G. has set to be the watchman to watch for your souls , coun counsel & exhort you in the name of G. to avoid all corrupt all vain profane unclean vain & Corrupt conversa. & accustom your selves when one with another to [-] such discourse as may be profitable & by which you may promote the good of Each others souls. you are come here this day to hear what Counsels & direction I have to give you from the w. of G. & by coming here you do as it were say that you desire to be Instructed & directed in the way of your duty . you came for that End & that you desire that you I whom am your Pastor should tell you what you ought to do for your souls Good . And this is the advice that I have to give you which is you ought to Recieve as a Counsel from X hims. because it is agreable to the mind & to the word of X & that which certainly is whole- [^003-note14] som for your soul & I may the Rather Expect that you will hearken to me because I Know that many of you making a profession of Loving G. above & X above all things in the Θ & of having devoted your selves to the service of G. & the business of Relig. Theref. Let me pray you to hearken to some things that I have to say to Lay before you as motives to stir up you up to comply with this Exh. Counsel to accustom your selves when together to such Conv Communication as has been spoken of.

_____________________________

1. Consid. that the Good of the soul is of Infinitely the Greatest Concern.

_________________________

2. speech is one Great thing whereby G. has distinguished us from the brute Crea. & set us above them & tis theref. most Reasonable that we should Improve it for him

__________________________________________________

3. Hereby you will promote Each others profit & ple your own & Each others ple profit & pleasure both together . This will be the most profitable discourse & not only so but it w you will find it the most pleasant . it would make your company & conversa. together pleasant [^003-note15] much more pleasant than to fill it up with vanity . with foolish & Lewd Jesting . talking against others spending away the time in that which turns serves to no purpose but only to take off the Heart from every thing that is Good. such conversa. would be pleasant because you woul it would be profitable the profit that you would find in it would make you Love it & take delight in it . it wi

________________

It would be pleasant because it would naturally tend to unite you in a vertuous friendship one with another no Con discourse or Conversation that so unites persons together as Religious discourse that by which they may Instruct & profit each others souls it naturally tends to Knit persons together in friendship . & the Reason is that the where such discourse is carried on there the God & of Love & peace is present there is X among them the prince of Love & Peace.

______________________________________________________

4. This is the way to Keep a Good Conscience 5 4. If you follow this advice then your Company [^003-note16] wont indispose you to the duty of secret prayer.

______________________________________________________

6 5. Then you will be in the way of the blessing of G. in your Company & in all other Concerns see that blessed text of SS. in Mal. 2. 16. 17.

_________________________________________________

________________________________________________

________________________________________________

7 6. Then you wont Repent you of your discourse & conversa. afterwards when you practice Corrupt con use corrupt discourse vain . -- unclean . you expect to Repent you Intend it

______________________________________________________

8 7. This is the way not to Lay a founda. in youth for Calamities & sorrows all your life after Job says. Job. 13. 16. thou writest bitter things against me thou makest me to possess the sins of my youth . [^003-note17] seeing theref. there are so many things to motives to induce you to hearken to this Counsel Let me [-] Insist upon it with you that are here present . when you come together Let X have a place among dont wholly shut him out of your Company he is ready to be one of you you bent too Good to admit him into your Companyes yea Let me advise often to take him with [-] you . he will do hurt in Company he wont spoil it but will make it every way better more profitable & more pleasant. Is it not a Great & Infinite Condescension in so Glorious a Person that he will vouchsafe. ----- ---- is it not an unspeakable honourable

_________

if you will admit X there will be his Love & his Joy power & his Joy among you that will spoil no Company & it not only will be with you then but it will follow you home & it will Remain with you oftentimes afterwards Be exhorted to make trial [^003-note18] you have heretofore used your self ------- --- why have you left it off . did it do you any hurt --- did it spoil your Company -------- does it is it better with you since you have Left it off . -- was G. a wildern. to you or a Land of darkn. that you have forsaken him.

____________

It may be all that now hear me wont h comply with this Exh. & it may be but a very few of this society will comply . I would theref advice those few that are willing to hearken to as much as

___________________

1. as much as they can to avoid the society of those that use thems. to corrupt communication & wont hearken.

____________

2. seek out such Company where profitable & Relig. -- Let those few that are willing associate t together ---- if others mock & say tis only pride & hypocrisy dont Regard . G. will observe G it G. will hearken & hear & a book of Remembrance will be written see if you cant find out companions that [^003-note19] will fall in with this advice & be much with them & X will be with you.

________________________

3. Let those that you choose for your speci peculiar Intim special friends & peculiar friends be those that are lively in Relig & delight & that delight in Relig Conversa. make of our special friends may converse freely without fear or suspicion one of another without suspecting one another of pride & hypocrisy . & tis more natural for Intimate friends to unburden thems. one to another.

_______________________________________

4. Take heed after you have begun not Gradually to Leave off . satan will Envy your happiness -- you have corruptions that will oppose ------- there will be many snares but take heed of the beginnings --- Keep up this practise with Great Care & watchfulness

This is what X speaks of in the two foregoing verses foregoing the text. for I am come to set a m. at variance against his F. and the d --------------------------------------------

and c theref. X says as he does in the text He that Loveth F -------------------------- he would give warning to such Children as are his [^005-note1] fo Profess to his followers that if w that if it should be so that they should have that trial that they should their Parents should threaten to disinherit them & turn them out of doors yea & deliv em up to death if they would not Renounce X that & Refuse to own em or treat em any more as their Chil yea should to turn Enemies to them if they would not renounce X I say X warns em that if in such a case they shew'd more Love to their F. than to him & so will Renou forsake him for fear of being Cast off by their Parents. He will renounce them at Last as not worthy men not worthy to be owned as some of his.

doc. Children ought to Love the L. Jesus X above ally things in this Θ.

_____________________________________________________

Tho when X says su in the Text he that Loves F. or mother more than me his words are not to be understood as Confined only to Ch those that are in a state of Childhood. or before they are Grown up. yet tis Evident he has a Special Respect to Chil. while they yet Remain under their parents Care for Roof & under their Covert & Care for he speaks of the division that shall be in housholds or families in the next v. before the text [^005-note2] He says a mans foes shall be them of his own houshold . & the words are Especially accommodated to the state of Childl Children while in a state of Childhoood when their affections of Chil. are wont to be strongest towards their Parents & it as having not been so much conversant abroad among other objects as after wards . Parents are very commonly the object of Childrens Greatest affections while young . So the latter part of the v. he that loveth son or d. more than me _____________ is Especially accommodated to the usual state of Persons after they are Grown up & are from[un]der their Parents Covert & have families of their own when Children are very commonly the dearest objects of their Love.

__________________________________________________

The following Reasons may be given why Chil. ought to Love J. X above things in the Θ 1. He is more Lovely in hims. He is one that is Greater and higher than all the Kings of the Earth has honour & majesty than they & yet he is Innately Good & full of mercy & Love.

_______________

### 564. Luke 22:32

### 564.Luke 22'32(8_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

__________________________________________________________________

Luke 22. 32. But I have prayed for thee that thy faith fail not . And when thou art Converted Streng -then thy Brethren . Christ is here foretelling Simon his shamefull fall in an hour of Temptation that night for this tho X said this to him the same night wherein he denied him Thrice. He He says to him in the v. before the Text . simon simon Satan --------------------- & then follow the words of the Text But I have prayed for thee ---------------------------------------- In these words I would observe two things

_______________________

1. something that Implies that Peter was already converted & that is what X says of his praying that his faith might not fail . which Implies that has had faith be tried and that faith which is not a temporary faith but a true faith that is a persevering faith for for tho it might have failed if X had not prayed this & promised that it should not fail . yet that he did pray that it should not fail shews that it was not a persevering faith for no faith is a persevering faith any other way than by Xs prayer & power. the faith of the best saint would soon be everthrown if it were not for the Intercession & strength of him who is the object of faith . There are many things that make it very manifest manifest that Peter was truly Conver savingly Con verted before now . on occasion of Peters professing [^006-note1] his faith in X that he w as the son of G. when he asked the d. who they thought that he was . X replied to him blessed art thou Simon Barjona. for flesh ------ -------------------- and on that occasion gave him the Honourable name of Peter or a Rock & tells him that on this Rock he would build his Chh & the ------ ---------------- Math as in the 16 Chap of Math and upon another occasion when many of the seeming discip of X went back & walked no more with him & X said to the twelve will ye also go away. simon Peter answered To whom should we Go thou hast the words of Et. Life and we believe and are sure that thou art X the son of the living G. as Joh. 6. 67. 68. 69

all the twelve d. excepting Judas only were saving ly Converted before this time y as appears by Xs Prayer in the 17. of Joh. particularly. v. 6. 7. 8 ------------------------------------------------ & & v 12 ---------------------------------------- Peter was not only a true Conv. before this but he seems to have been one of the most Eminent of them Peter & James & John on several occasions that the Apostle Paul speaks of the Pillars after Xs Resurrection Gal. 2. 9. seemed to have been upon many occasions distinguished from the Rest by X before his death . . so it was these that were admitted to be present at Xs transfigura. & it was these three only that were admitted to be present when he Raised Jairius d. & it was [^006-note2] these three that X took with him in the time of his Agonies . which plainly shewed a Preference of Pet. & James & John . above the Rest of the d. But

Repent ye therefore & be converted that your sins may be blotted out. & the like in other places . & so

If they have obtained a saving change yet they are at a very Great distance from what they should be the Kingd. of Heaven within them is but as a Grai of Grain of mustardseed . they still need to s Pass under a very Great Change & tis what they should theref still be seeking after with no Less diligence than they sought the first saving Change .

And indeed for Persons as soon as they have obtained an hope that they are Converted [-] presently to Grow more slack in Relig. & Less engaged in their minds about it & more remiss in their Endeavours is the way for em soon to Come to those Circumstances wherein Persons above all others stand in need of a second Conversion renewed Conversion . [^006-note35] tis for tis the way for em by degrees to sink into a very carnal dead carnal unXtian frame of sp. and have their Corruptions prevail more & more 'till they carry them away as a flood .

__________________________________________________________________

[-] There is sometimes a Great deal of talk of Converison amongst us but tis pity there is no more talk of Godly per meaning the Conversion of natural men but tis pity there is no more of Godly persons Conversion . There is doubtless a Great deal of need of it many there is a gross by the General appearance of the state of Religion in this there are a Great many that have some Grace in their hearts that do in a stand in very Great & special need of being converted & tis pity that such is a conversion is not more thought of & talked of, & sought after.

__________________________________________________________________

2. Hence we may Learn how that Godly Persons may apply to thems. & Improve for their own Good what they hear preached especially to natural men. . [--] There is but little that is said in sermons to natural men but few Exhorta that are given but what Godly [^006-note36] men may very properly hear for thems . There are but few Exhorta. that are given to natural men from the Pulpit but what Godly men may apply to thems. as in many Respects suiting their Case. If the minister Endeavours to shew me natural men the dreadfull nature Guilt that sin brings & the dreadfullness of the wrath of G. G. to awaken them Godly Persons need to be awakened as well as they & to be a They also need to be convinced more of the Great evil of sin & the dreaf dreadfullness of Gods wr.

If that be delivered in a sermon that is to Convince natural men of the utter insufficiency of their own Righ. this the Godly may take to thems. for they they also need to be convinced of the Insufficiency -----------------

_________

utter helplessness

___________

if they are Exhorted Earnestly to seek Conversion

___________

If they are Exhorted to strive in order to it to be violent for the K. of Heaven to follow on to Know the L. ----------- [^006-note37]

There are fe but few of those directions that are given from the Pulpit to natural men in order to their Conversion but what are proper for the Godly to attend & follow in seeking a renewed Conversion . Christ & his apostles commonly were not wont to make any distinction between saints & sinners in those Exhorta. & directions that they gave to others seek salvation Gods Grace & the salvation of their souls . In the 18. of Math X says to his discip. Except ye be conv. ---- he did --------------- he did not direct hims. only to Judas . so when he says in the 5 of Math 20 v. Except your Righ. shall exceed the Righ. of the scribes --------- he speak speaks to his d. as you may see by the beginning of the Chap. . so he says to em again in the 25 ------------------------- -------------------------------- which is a direction in order to Escape hell so again 29. 30 v -----------------------------

so X in the same sermon wherein he is [^006-note38] still speaking to the disciples in the 7. Chap. 13. 14. v. -----------------------------------

and so the Apostles from time to time in their Epistles give rules directions what those that they write to shall should do for their salva. & yet write to those that they have Charity for as saints & faithfull in X. Jesus.

__________________________________________________________________

3. Here we may Learn that there are but few Petitions that are proper to be put up to G. but what are also pr by natural men but what are also proper for the Godly . some Persons that have had hope that they were Converted but are very much at a loss but are full of doubts & fears complain that they are at a loss how to form their petitions whether they should pray to G. as natu those that are Unconverted or as those that are Converted but the difficulty is so great as some imagine m [-] for whether they are Converted or unconverted most of those requests that are proper to be put up by an unconverted man are proper for them Let em pray that G. would give h them his sp. to a- [^006-note39] waken them this is proper for them whether they are Conv have any true Grace or not & so Let pray that G. would enable to strive for salva. & that he would help em to Enter in at the strait Gate. Let em pray Earnestly that G. would shew em their own hearts -- bring off from own Righ. ----- G. would convert em -- Give a true sight of X ---- & Enable 'em sincerely to close with him & trust in him alone for salvation . Let em pray that G. would raise them open their blind eye ------- cause Light to shine out of darkness ----- & that he raise their dead souls to life . such Petitions such Petitions are proper to be put up to G. by a minister as one that speaks as the mouth of a Congregation & they are proper petitions to be join'd in by the whole Congregation both saints & sinners.

__________________________________________________________________

4. Hence we may Learn what is the proper [-] course for them to take in order to satisfaction about their good Estate that are much exercised with doubts about their Good Estate through their having been Long in dead & Carnal frames [^006-note40] viz. Earnestly to seek that they may be Converted . when those that are truly Godly have fallen into such frames & have Long conti nued in them the almost certain & Infallible Conseq. of it Great doubts about their Good Estate when Grace is so buried with corruption it wont be easily seen & there will be many doubts whether there be any

________________

now this is the proper Course to be taken in such a Case in order to satisfaction about their Good Estate is to strive to enter in at the strait Gate & to be violent for the Kingd. of H. to [-] strive for Con & Cry to G. for his Converting Grace. to cry after wisd. & lift up their vioce for understanding ------

__________________

very Commonly the Course that is taken in such cases to R in order to a Resolution of the doubts that arise is to try thems. by signs & indeed that ought not to be left undone but when that Course alone is taken it almost universall it almost universally proves in vain . unless there be a new w. of Gods sp. to Lift the soul out of [^006-note41] its dead Carnal frame. all the best & signs . & the most searching trying discourses are found in vain the doubts will Remain

_____________

The properest & best course for all such persons to take to be delivered from their doubts is Immediately without delay to apply thems. to obtain the Grace of G. opening their blind Eyes & quickening their dead souls as Earnestly as if they Knew they never h yet had recieved any thing of it & if this Course was more Earnestly taken instead of spending a Great deal time in examining & Looking over the first work & past experiences. there would be fewer complaints of doubts & fears than now there ar among Professours than now there is .

______________________________________________________

II. Use may be of Exh. to the Godly such as have had hope that they have been the subjects of a saving Change yet notwithstanding Earnestly to seek that they may be Converted . I would direct [-] this exh. to all such but for as has been shown tis proper for all but Especially exhort such to it [ ] in whom grace if they have any is very Low [^006-note42] & such as have been Long in ill frames dead & carnal frames of sp. & have not behaved thems. as becomes Xtians . Consid . what has been said of the Great need you stand in of a new Change . & further to stir you up to seek it consider two things .

_________________________

1. when the Godly are thus through Gods Grace the subjects of a new Change it Commonly brings in em into much more Excellent Happy Circumstances than their first Change when G. works a second work it shall not be for nothing he he will [ ] carry the soul by it he commonly carr thereby carries the soul to a much Greater height in holiness than ever it was brought to before. to much Greater degrees of the Kn. of G. to a firmer faith to be more rooted & Grounded in Love. to have more the mastery of the corruptions of the Hearts & to have Greater strength against tempta & to serve G. in a more steady even manner . & to Enjoy a mos more settled Peace of Consc. to live more constantly comfortably in the Enjoymt of much more of [^006-note43] the Light of Gods Countenance

______________________

what a blessed alteration was there in Peter after that Conversion that is spoken of in the text . He that was then so Cowardly so afraid to own his master . how Courageous does he appeard after his Recovery . how bold in the Cause of X & how full of the sp. of G. in does he appear to be by what we read of him in the 2d & following Chapters of the Acts.

_________________________

2. Persons after such a [ ] new Godly Persons after such a renewed work of Gods sp. upon them are uni commonly not only in a much happier state thems. but much more able to help others . hereby their Knowl & Exp & strength is Greatly increased and having been tempted & delivered they are the more fit to help others under temptation & by their Great access which they have Recieved of Light & strength thems. they are abundantly the more fit to Instruct & strengthen others hence X the direction that X g Gives to Peter in the text when thou art conv. streng- [^006-note44] then thy Br. so that if you obtain such a change t by a new w of the sp of G. upon your heart you are not only likely to be a much happier man your self but to be a much Greater blessing to all that are About you & others are like to be the happier for it, as well as you.

### 568. Ps. 119:56

### 568.Ps. 119'56(10_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

__________________________________________________________________

Ps.119. 56. This I had because I kept thy Precepts

___________

There are two thing in this. 1 davids strict & observance of Gods Commands . Thus much is Thus much is implied in the Expression of Keep I Kept thy precepts. The[-] Expression is very significant. there are many SS. Phrases that through common use without attending to the natu ral import of them Loose through three quarters of their significancy . we having alwaies been used to the expressions without explications are habi tually inattentive to the proper meaning so it is with Respect to that SS Phrase of denying our selves . & so it is with that of Keeping Gods Commandmt. which properly imports something more than some sort of Comforming to a Command in some in Gods Commands in some times it naturally implies a firm a constant strict & firm Cleaving to a Command as a thing that we Keep & will not Let Go & t or as tak a thing Com a treasure Commu ted to us to Keep that we are carefull not to Loose or Let slip from us by any means & that we resolutely hold & wont suffer any one to [-] Get it away from us

________________________

Gods Command is a Gods Law is as it were a treasure that he has Committed to us to Keep and meerly a Conforming to that Law in now & then in some instances is far far falls far short of Keeping of it according to SS. Ph the use of the SS. Phrase a man may now then have a Jewel in his hand & may take up sometimes upon occasion & to but be far from Keeping it [-] he may be very careless of it & often drop & Easil or Easy Easily suffer it to be got away from him by that that have a mind to deprive He only Keeps the Jewel that carefully lays it up that watches diligently Least it be stollen that holds it Resolutely & strongly against those that struggle with him for it. & so Constantly retains it that he may safely Return it at Last to him that committed it to him

_______________

so that the Phrase of Keeping Gods Commands signi fies a resolute strict & constant cleaving to Gods Commands or holding them fast & especially under trials & when we are tempted to Let them go or depart from them as the phrase [^007-note1] in the 31. v. of this Ps. I have stuck to thy testimonies david when he speaks of the text of his having Kept Gods precepts probably has Respect to several seasons instances wherein he had Remarkeable temptations to depart from them he speaks in the fo verses next foregoing of a state of trial that he hadbeen in thy statutes have been my songs in the House of my Pilgrimage I remembred thy name O L. in the night and have Kept thy Law. by his Pilgrimage he Probably means that wandering state that he was in [-] during sauls persecution when he banished and had no fixed habitation but wandered about from place to place as a st Pilgrim or stranger & sometimes sojourned in a strange Land in the Land as he sojourned as a stranger once & again in the Land of the Philistines. or possibly it may have Respect to his wandering & sojourning beyond yond Jordan when driven away by his son absalom.

________________

This time This perhap dark time probably may be what he means by the night in the verse next before the Text wherein it is said [^007-note2] he Remembred Gods name & Kept his Law

______

This time was a time of Great trial to david & he had many remarkeable temptations in it to Let Gods Law precepts but he Kept them & stuck to them.

_________________________

2. Here it is a benefit The other thing in the Text is the benefit he had by this his sticking to Gods Prec Precepts. This I had is as we particularly said what but the manner of expression seems to import two things concerning it . 1. That it was something that david highly prized that he thought & of & Enjoyed with delight & pleasure something that was excellent & sweet to him this Excellent fruit this sweet Reward had I. there was something that he had that he thought worthy of a special notice & & peculiar Remark & a reward to be made of it .

________

2. That it was something that [--] ceased to Look back with approbation & pleasure on his so finally adhering to Gods Commands and [^007-note3] on the account of which he was gl fully approved & Rejoiced in his Conduct therein & was Glad that he did not listen to the temptation he had to depart from Gods Law.

________________________________________________

doc. when Person have been strictly to their duty in any Instances wherein they have been much tempted to depart from it they commonly afterwards find that Benefit & Reward of it even in this Θ that makes Glad that they have done so.

________________________________________________

The method in which I would handle this observationis this . 1. I would take notice of the several Cases of trial & opposition wherein if Persons Cleave strictly to their duty it is wor they are wont to meet with such as them a fruit of it

_________

2. I would observe what Kind of Benefits & Rewards Persons are wont to meet with in this Θ after they have thus stuck to their duty .

_________

3. I would give the Reasons their meeting with such Benefits [--] make them Re- [^007-note4] joice in it that they have been strict to their duty. 1. It is commonly thus when persons stick to their duty, Contrary to strong Inclinations of their nature . so it was with Abraham when he was Commanded to offer up to G. his son his only son Isaac upon the Altar. to Kill with his own hands that beloved son of his the son of his old age the only son of Sarah his beloved wife the son of his hopes & whence he according to Gods Promise expected such a numerous offspring & all those blessings to & by his seed. what could be more Contrary to the inclination of a tender Loving Father than with his own hands to take the Knife to cut the throat of his own dear dutifull Child. Bu

______________

But yet in this instance Abraham stuck to the Command of G. he would not hearken to the Earnestly sollicitations of his natural affections he turned a deaf ear to the voice of his tender affections & complied fully with Gods Commands he went with his son to the place appointed he built up the altar & with his own hands bound his son Isaac & Laid him [^007-note5] on the altar & took the Knife and was Going to Give the mortal stroke his hand was stretched out & was actually in the motion which if God had not staid would have done the [-] mortal execution so that he did in Effect & in his own thought & Intention & action [-] slain his sl h slay his actu slay his son it was not for want of any thing in him but from an expressed Restraint prevention from Go that it was not done .

________________

Bu And how much did he meet with to make him Rejoice that he was he was strictly followed Gods Command in this matter . G. Returned his son to him with many Gracious manifestations of his favour beyond & abundant promises of future mercy to His Posterity by Isaac beyond all that ever he had Recieved before as you may see in the 22. Chap. of Genesis Gen. so that tho he went forth weeping to offer the Land of Moriah there to offer his son it was doubtless heavy work to him causing an Exceeding weight on his sp. yet he Returned again with Rejoicing with Isaac his son & Gods blessing added hims. & him his son as it were risen from the dead & Gods blessing added to hims. & him ________________________________________________ & when Persons Cleave to their duty when they are tempted from it by some violent l their Carnal appetites when the bait is set be fore them & they are tempted with a fair oppor tunity to Gratify their Lusts & take their swing in Iniq. & does utterly & utterly leave as when young persons in the heat of youth repel strong temptations that they have to unclean ness they are wont to meet with something after wards that makes em Glad that they have done so. so it was Thus Joseph when he in his youth he had opportunity of a Carnal Enjoymt of one much over of much higher place in the Θ even his own mistress the Potiphars Lady that was a Great man in Egypt he wh & not only [--] had Opportunity but [----] was tempted & sollicited from day to day yea & had Laid hold of Joseph to yea and was Laid hold of by her in in a very bld bold & impudent manner . yet he utterly Rejected the temptation he stuck fast to Gods command he would by no means do that Great wickedness & sin against G. [^007-note6] & how did G. Reward him for it afterwards in that his Great wealth & advancemt in [-] Egypt giving him one of the most a yet more honourable Family in Egypt to be his wife

________________________________________________

so Ruth when she forsook her own native Countrey & mothers House & all her old a Relations & old acquaintances & then & all her former pleasant Enjoymts in that Land she must we to Go to the Land of Israel to put her trust under the wings of the G. of Israel she must needs Go greatly against her her natural Inclination . Naomi put her in mind of the diffi culty of it once & again . yet she Resolute ly Cleaved to Naomi & stuck to her duty she said to Naomi Intreat me not to Leave or to Return from following after thee for whither thou Goest I will Go & where thou Lodgest I will Lodge thy People shall be my People & thy G. my G. & G. have her such a Reward by bringing into such a Circumstances in Israel & made her house famous in Bethlehem in her Lifetime & afterwards made her family the Royal family of Israel. for she was the from her came david & [--] aftewards he the Root & offspring of david J. X. [^007-note7]

2. It is commonly so when persons Cleave fast to Gods Commands tho for the present they suffer it be an occasion of much suffering. Indeed when persons suffer death in the Cause of X there is no opportunity for them to Recieve any [-] Reward for it in this Θ that should make em afterwards Rejoice that they have done so. But when they are called to other sufferings & tho their Reward will be the more abundant in another Θ But when they undergo other sufferings Rather than forsake Gods Commands & th they com they commonly find some Bene fit by it in this Θ that makes em rejoicein it that they have so done.

so Joseph by so resolutely resisting his mis tresses temptation not only forewent did not only forgo these sinful pleasures which she temp ted him to but occasion was an occasion of of the displeasure of his master of his falling under the displeasure of his master & his being cast into prison & even into the dungeon & his Lying there many years . . But yet such was the Consequence at Length that this was an occasion of even in this Θ for he was exalted out of the dungeon to be a p the state of a prince over all the Land of Egypt & it was was an occasion of a remote occasion of his again seeing his Father & his Brethren [-] that he had been so long separated from so daniel the three Chil. by exposing thems. to suff. in Babilon were advanced to Great honour & high Estate there X tells his disciples in the 18 of Luke 29 & 30 verses that he that has Left house or Parents or Brethren or wife or children for the King. of Gods sake shall recieve manifold more in this present time besides everlasting life in the Θ to come.

3. It when Persons obey Gods Comman follow Gods Comma God in the dark without being able fo to foresee or Conjecture particularly what the Event will be they Commonl of are wont afterward to meet with that make em Rejoice in it that they have so done . it is found in the Issue to be safe following Gods direction tho he directs us to Go we Know not whither and tho he Leads us in such a way that we [^007-note8] cant see a step before us and tho as to any thing that appears to our human view of things we dont Know but that the next step we shall fall into a pit or fall down some precipice. Persons that sti strictly Cleave to G. commonly rejoice in it afterw. th tho they [-] when they did so they could things Looked so dark to them that they could not so much as conjecture what the Event would be. so it was with Abraham when G. bid him Get him out of his own countrey & from his F. House into a Countrey that he should shew him of. he did not tell him what Coun trey but only a Countrey that He shou would shew him of He tho it was doubtless very hard to Abraham to forsake ---------------- but yet he went not Knowing whither he went as Heb. 11 8 and but the Event was such that Abraha Abraham Received such abundant mercy in that Land that he had Great Cause afterw. to Rejoice in [^007-note9] it that he had done as G. commanded him

after he came into that Land G. promised to Give him that Land but it was a Long time before he told him how when he first came there he gave him none of it no not so uch as to set his foot on. acts 7. 5.

& the all the possession that ever he had there was am during his life time was only the possession of a burying place a possession for him to be in after he was dead & not a possession to live on.

Led the Chil. of Israel out of Egypt [^007-note10] he Led Led em into a Great wildern. where they Knew not the way they did G had not told em how he intended to bring em into Canaan or which way they should come thither & in all the way they went they went they Knew not which way whi ther they were going but yet they sh such was the Event to those that fully followed the L. & Cleaved Close to him that they had Great Cause to Rejoice in it -------------

4. Persons are wont afterwards to meet with that that makes em Rejoice that they [-] have Kept Gods C. --- when there so doing in the time of it seems to be very dangerous & when if they have strictly [-] attended their duty when when as to what appears to hum an Human view of things there seems to be no prospect of its being to their advantage but many ways to their hurt. & the most probable Event so far as Can be Conjectured from Circumstances that already appear there seems to be nothing but Rocks & precipices in the way . so it was with Caleb & Joshua if they [---] followed G. to Canaan to Endeavour [^007-note11] to take possession of that Land there appeared nothing but Giants & Giants in the way they had been to Canaan & had seen their Cities wall'd up to Heaven high walls & had seen the sons of Anak there in whose sight they were but as Grasshoppers so that there was no prospect of any thing Else than of their being destroy Qu an Easy prey to them & Quickly destroyed if they went to Encounter em any otherwise than as they trusted in God trust & hoped for his help to figh for them to be [-] their side for them

but G. had commanded em to Go & theref they were by all means for going they wholly followed the L. & strictly obeyed his Commands & such was the Issue of things such were the Great Benefits & Rewards they met with for their strict obedience that they had Great Cause to Rejoice in the End that they had so done one of them was advanced to Great honour made Captain of ------------ & G. have made him the Glorious Conqueror -------------- & favoured him with extraordinary tokens of [^007-note12] his favour --- Conversed with him as he had done with moses . ---- & the other G. Gave a distinguishing inheri tance in that Land some of the best of the Land one of the principal Cities & made him a remarkable Conqueror over those very sons of Anak that others so much feard Josh. 15. 13. &c-------

so when the Chil. of Israel were come to the Red Sea. & the wilderness had shut them [-] in they could not Go to the Right hand nor Left & the Egyptians their Enemies were behind them & the Red Sea was before them & they Knew not what to do & Cried to G. for help . G. bid em Go forward. Exod. 15 14. 15. And the L. said unto moses wheref criest thou unto me? speak unto the Chil. of Isra el that they go forward . But alass how could they go forward to go that way appeared most dangerous for there was the sea before wh if they went forward they did not [-] see how they should should do any other than Go directly into the [^007-note13] d sea . but yet this was Gods Command & however dangerous it appeared yet the event was such that they had Great Cause of Rejoicing that they had obeyed that Command tho they went in fearing & trembling yet when they came out on the opposite shore it was with singing & Rejoicing singing the song of moses & playing with timbrels & Instruments of musick

so Abrhaam when he was 5. when Persons are strict [-] in obeying Gods Command . they [--] at Last meet with that makes em Rejoice in it tho for a Considerable time after it seems as tho it proved it seems to be only to their disadvantage it not only appea the way of obedience se not only seems dangerous beforehand but tending to make them fear whether it [-] wont prove for their hurt but [-] afterewards for some time it seems to prove to be to their hurt. they meet with no Reward no sensi ble benefit for some time & indeed only but trouble Loss & trouble as the Conse- [^007-note14] quence but yet finally the Event of things in such they find the Consequence to be so tempt so they are tempted to Repent that they had denied thems. & been so [-] strict in their obedience as they had be were & to think that tis in vain & that they have wronged thems. by it & say as the Psalmist Ps. 73. 13. 14. verily I have Cleansed my heart in vain & washed my hands in innocency for all the day long have I been plagued & Chas tened every morning . but yet as they and as Elisha when he was persecuted for do ing his duty 2. K. 6. 33. Behold this Evil is of the L. what should I wait for the L. any Longer . yet by waiting patiently on the L. & still Cleaving to him they are wont finally to find the Conseq. to be such as to Cause em Greatly to Rejoice that they did obeyed Gods commands notwithstanding all the difficulties that seemed to be Consequent for a while

so it was with Abraham when G. had bid him Get out of his own Countrey into a Land that he should shew him of not telling him what Land he sho it should be & [^007-note15] he went did as G. bid him & forsook his own Countrey & his Fathers house & all that was dear to him there & [---] went out not Knowing whither he went & came into the Land of Canaan the Land that G. Led him to . But presently after But when Abraham first came into the Land there was a Grievous Famine in the Land & very soon after as you may see in the 12. Chap. of Gen. this was poor Encouragemt to Abraham. to think that he had forever forsaken his native Countrey Countrey & all his Relations there to come into this Land hoping to find it a pleasant fruitfull Land and to Enjoy all manner of blessings in it & when he but instead of that when he came to find a countrey burnt up with drought & instead of plenty to find a Grievous famine & want of all things & immediately to be driven out of it again & be forced to fly the Countrey as soon as he came into it & to Go down into Egypt for sustenance this was a very Great trial to [^007-note16] Abraham & Looked as tho the way of Gods C. was not the way to prosperity but yet by waiting on G. he Received both those sp. & temporal blessings in that Land that gave him Great Cause to Rejoice that he had obeyed & followed & followed G. as he had done so it was with Joseph when he Refused to Comply with his mistresses tempta. & if the Benefit bent Evidently occasioned by the obed act of obed. yet it is of such a nature as manifestly shows a Relation to it as when Caleb Cleav the Chil. of Israel murmured by Reason of what the spies told em of the sons of Anak. that they supposed it was impossible for 'em to Conquer but Caleb [-] still Cleaved to G. & trusted in him strength that they should be able by his help in his strength Easily to Conquer & Caleb afterwards was Rewarded by being made s making him be chief conqu of those very sons of Anak & Giving Hebron the City where they principally dwelt for his Possession. the Reward had such an Evident Relation to the deed that it declared itself to be a Reward of it.

They were wont the Benefits they Recieve of their strict obed. makes 'em Rejoice in their obed strictness. is that their Reward what i carries in it that testimony of Gods favour that is better to them than the thing it self that is the fruit of it a little Temporal Good Given [--] as a token of Gods Love is better than vast Revenues & Riches [^007-note49] without. when Persons have strictly obeyed G. under Great trial G. is often wont to bestow some Benefit upon them with Evident manifesta. of his favour. as makes it Exceeding sweet. . sometimes the Benefit comes in a very unexpected manner so there seems to be a visible hand of G. in it 8 & we can ascribe it to nothing but divine provid & it is it such a a Benefit given on fulfillmt of Gracious promises made to the Righ. whi is such a Benefit as was promised & in such a way as was promised & with faith to wh obey the soul is help from G. to enable at the same time to act faith in the promises & thankfullness to praise his Re him for his rich & undeserved mercy.

not of a But when difficulties and oppositions arise which are the proper trials of obedience they w make but a poor hand of it they will strangely twist & turn to save thems. when they come to see a cross Lying in the way many contri vances will then they dont Go right forward with a steady [--------] strait Course as they did before but they beg into go more slowly & sometimes stop & then they will turn to the Right hand & sometimes to the Left many Contrivances there in to shift the Cross oftentimes they will be Looking about for some body Else to take it of up to take the bur s they are for putting of it off to others they think it more properly belongs to them [^007-note51]

if they do something they will be sure to do little enough they are extraordinary carefull Least they should hurt thems & what is done it not that has any difficulty in it [-] is not done with cheerfullness they are as it were drove or dragg'd to it . neither have they a sp. to trust in G. in the dark G. makes many Promises to them how that if they will trust him & will for the present forego their temporal Interest he will take care in his own time & way to make it up to them but they want to see how they want that they dont care to trust to Gods meer word they want to see some likely way of their gaining by doing a di their duty a difficult duty before they do in short they want to have a diffi cult made not a difficult duty before they consent to comply with it they bent bent [^007-note53] of a sp. to Run any Great ventures for G. nor do they care to trust G. any further than they can see him men Love to Know how their Interest may be secured beforehand they dont care to Leave that matter wholly with G.

& if they have done something that they think is notable they are soon out of patience in waiting for their Reward if they wait a little while & dont see that they get any thing by it but on the contrary it seems to be to their disadvantage they quickly draw up a Conclusion that there is nothing to be got by exposing ones self to difficulty & suffering for G. if they are perswaded for a little while to go on travel in the diffiuclt road of Laborious selfdenying Xtianity they are soon out of breath they [--] are impatient to have G. give em something to make up for the difficulty they undergo other wise they [-] Grow quite weary of it & soon Leave it off. [-] that they have no more of a sp. to suff. for G. & to trust in his promise for a Reward & patiently to to [----] continue patiently to Endure & patiently to wait for the Reward for tis patience that works exp. as the apostle tells us in Rom. 5. 3. 2. Hence we may Learn how much otherwise it is with the ways of strict obed from what it is with the ways of sin for wheras there are such Great Benefits in the fruits of the former there is nothing to be Got but wo & misery by the Latter. The ways of sin diff for the present are are Easy they are agreable to the Inclinations of nature tis down hill & the way that our heavy molded flesh & blood naturally sways us. tis a way of sloth & indolence & [^007-note56] pleasure a way strewed with flowers. wheras the way of strict obed is the up hill is by a steep ascent & is full of Rocks & briars & thorns But herein the ways of sin difer from ways of strict obed. that there is nothing to be Got by them in their Issue there is flowers without fruit & these flowers are but shadows when they wither away with a touch. men in these ways are constantly persuing shadows [-] some appearance that promises fair at a distance but never yields what it promises Rom. 6. 21. what fruit had ye then of those things whereof ye are now ashamed for the End of those things is death as much as if the Apostle had said who Look back & Consid. what have you got by all those ways of wickedness that you heretofore have so Eagerly committed . what [-] benefit have you Recieved by all are you have you Ever [---] the happier for them. hant you served sin & done satans drudgery for nothing are & are you not ashamed of your folly in it when you Consider it There is nothing to Get by ways of sin for the [^007-note57] soul. nothing [-] feeds that or answers its necessities on the Contrary tis a m sin is a mortal prison it it and there is nothing that is worth the having that is to be got forthe body that is worth the having men by ways of sin seek to promote advance their temporal Interest in ple pleasure Profit & honour but how often is the Reverse of those the Issue of it [--]instead of obtaining honour how often do men bring thems to [-] shame & contempt by it & [-] instead of Riches how commonly is it so that those that have Least value bring thems. to poverty & run in debt & come to the Jail. & instead of pleasure living in pleasantly in the Θ they are very how often do they come to such bring thems. to diseases pains & death

so that if wicked men seem to Get any thing by their wickedness they indeed Get nothing at all they are like the Partridge that sitteth on Eggs & Haches them not notwithstanding all their [-] not seem Gain by sin in the End they shall appear to be fools [--] that have served sin for nothing. II. Use is of Exhorta. Re firmly & strictly to Cleave to the way of duty under all temptations and opposition whatever difficulty opposition or difficulty it be attended with. if your duty seems to abridge you very much of the Enjoymt of those sensual delights that you have a strong Inclination to and it would be a cultin or if there be any particular Enjoymt that is very sweet to you the any that you must part with any Pleasant morsel that that you can't bear the thoughts of parting [^007-note62] with or any Pleasant morsel that you hold in your mouth & are very Loth to spit out yet Remember what has been said dont in dont in such Cases do as commonly persons do Go about to find out excuses & study for [-] arguments to [-] Justify your allowance of the Indulgence of your Inclination. don't hide the morsel under your tongue and if it be [-] your right Eye yet dont spare it but Lay violent hands upon it dont in such Cases treat your self with tenderness and handle your Lusts as if you was very Loth to hurt em and it grieved you to deal hardly with them you must have no mercy on them come to that to have no mercy on them any more any more than the Chil. of Israel had on Achan whom they brought forth & he & his Chil. & all his oxen & his asses & all that he had & burnt them [-] with fire & stoned them with stones & not only so but burnt them with fire & raised a Gr & so utterly extirpated crucifying is a cruel death to put any to but you must put your Lusts to such a death you must crucify the [^007-note63] flesh with the aff -------- -------- Gal. 5. 24.

things upon your self in in even [-] In this Θ tho the flower be [--] gay & fair & pleasant the fruit when it is ripe is Gall & worm is as the Cruel venom of asps is Gall & wormwood & as the Cruel venom of asps. but if you will strictly Cleave to Gods Com. & avoid all appearance of Evil & even those Practices that tend & Expose to sin the fruit will be sweet & the Gain Great you will in all probability be Rewarded an hundred fold in this Life again if you see any opportunity of Great Gain again if the way of duty at any time seems to [^007-note64] Lead to suffering if [--] it seems to expose to suffer Reproach & Contempt if there be any young Person the here that is under that tempta. to sen licen tiousness that if he don't do as other young people do dont comply with them in their singular if you are bold & Resolute in it that you will Cleave fast to the way of duty Let the Conseq. be what it will you will afterwards Rejoice in it & in all pro yea this is the likeliest way for you to be had in honour if it seems to throw into a dungeon for theh present w that which appears as dark yea as Josephs dungeon for the present yet at Last you it will prove your advance mt & with a Crown of honour upon your head what a Reward had Noah for being singular & venturing the Contempt & Reproach of all the Θ . what a Reward had Caleb & Joshua when they were alone & all the Congregation against 'em & even to that degree that they were ready to stone them with stones if the the way of duty Looks threatning & dangerous [^007-note65] as tho you should get nothing by it but & as if nothing but a train of difficulties & su & Loss & disadvantages would be the Conseq. yet Let me beseech you in nothing to be terrified by this dont Let such threatning appearances fright you from your duty but still Cleave fast to it not fearing the run the venture of it at Gods [-] Call. tis safe venturing through the swellings of Jordan or into the sea or a lions den if you do so you will see Cause to Rejoice in it after wards Be Perswaded to make trial prove G. herewith & see if he wont come & pour out a blessing upon you but only dont be in too much hast if the Reward dont seem presently to come but what have done in the way of duty seems for a while to be to your disadvantage dont be too short breathed soon to give out in discouragemt as tho the way of duty was not the way to Prosperity He Isai 28. 16. He that believes shall not make haste Continue to do good still tho it be und diff the diffi culty should seem to continue Long Trust in the L. & do good & verily you shall [^007-note66] be fed at Last with that which will be sweet . Continue to Go on in the way of your duty & s your selfdenying suffering duty & go on in it with Cheerfulness delight your self in the L. & you will find that at last he gives you the desires of your heart when his time comes he will bring forth your Right as the L. and your Judgment as the noon day . dont be in too much hast for your Reward but Rest in the L. & wait patiently for him and in a little while you shall delight your self in the abundance of Peace.

then come in the words of the text Except your Righ. ------------------------------------------ ] It was a time now when the Jews in General were big with expectations of that Great Kingdoms being set up in the Θ which the prophet daniel foretold when he said Chap 2. 44. in the days of these Kings the L. In discoursing from these words I would 1. show how far the Pharisees went in Relig. & 2. wh How our Righ. must Exceed theirs if would we would ever enter into the K. of H. [^008-note3] & then make some application

I. I would observe in some things how far the scribes & Pharisees went in Relig.

______________________________________________

1. many of them were men of a Great deal of libe ral Knowledge. the scribes as was but now observed were their divines devoted to study the study of divinity. & were their doctors or teachers of in matters of Religion. the Jews had their schools where their youth many of their youth were put to Learning under some Eminent doctor. such an one was Gamaliel un at whose feet Paul while he Rem when a Pharisee was brought up & out of these schools proceeded their Learned men or scribes that were teachers in their synagogues They the jews admir we Read that X at twelve years old was found in the Temple sitting in the midst of the doctors both hearing them & asking them questi ons these doctors were their scribes

the jews admired how X came by such Know & wisd as he discovered in his discourses having never Learned Joh. 7. 15. and the Jews marvelled saying how Knoweth this man Letters having [^008-note4] never learned they wondered how he came by his Learning having never been put to any of their schools of Learning as their scribes had been

___________________________________________

2. They were many of them very much in reading & studying the ss. Considerable part of E---- the External duty of Gods People Lies in reading & searching the ss. the P we are Commanded to search & read out of the Book of the L. to search the ss. the Plamists [-] often declares how conversant he was with the word of G. These scribes & Pharisees many of them abounded in this duty they made it very much their Business to study the Law by which they meant sometimes only the five books of moses sometimes the who all the Books of the Old T. which contained the whole written w. of G. that was then extant & from their study of the Law many of them were called Lawyers

________________________________________________

3. They were Exceeding strict in the Externals of the worship of G. they lived under a dispena. wherein They were strict in the External sanctifi cation of the sabbath . they were very strict in observing the festivals commanded in the Law of mo ses & in attending all the Rites of Gods w. in [^008-note5] the Temple. They lived under a dispensa. w wherein many more things were Required in the carrying on the External w. of G. than are now there was a vast multitude of very difficult & burdonsome rites & Ceremonies to be attended L Laws about their sacrifices & about tithes & about their Legal Purifications. besides those External their daily prayers & reading publick w. of G. in their synagogues. but the Pharisees were exceeding strict in attending them even to such the smallest punctilies such as tithing mint & annise & ----- & all the herbs in their Gardens there were no sect among the Jews so strict in these things as the Pharisees . ------------- Acts 26. 5. after the most strictest sect of our Relig. I lived a Pharisee

_______________________________________________

4. They were not only very strict to observe the Law. but went far beyond what was required of them in these things they were went beyond what was Required of them in their rigidness upon the sabbath day they condemned so much as plucking Ea Earns of Corn to ea when passing through a field to Eat when hungry & in Great want of food. they would not so much [^008-note6] as employ a Phisician to help the sick on the sabbath and least they should defile them selves & so make thems. unfit for the w. of G. they would not have no dealings with the samaritans whom they Looked upon as a polluted people & would not converse with publicans or Eat with them and others that were Looked upon as vicious & Immoral persons

& so carefull were they of their of external puri ty that they would by no means eat bread before they had first washed their hands and they abounded in acts of w. beyond what was Required of them they made Long Prayers that they might seem extraordinary devout & some of them fasted twice a week & made broad their Philacteries

They wore w- instead of wearing Gay Clothes as many of carnal as in the manner of many carnal men they were wont to some [^008-note7] of them at Least to wear hair Cloth G wear Hair Cloth to afflict thems. next their skin to afflict thems. which seems to have been the manner of hypocritical professours among the Jews of old. wh by Zech. 13. 4. and it shall come to pass in that day that the pro phets shall be ashamed every one of his vision when he hath prophecied neither shall they wear a rough Garment to decieve in the orig. it is a Garm of Hair. ### 573. II Cor. 2:15-16

### 573.II Cor. 2'15-16(10_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

__________________________________________________________________

2. Cor. 2. 15. 16. --- To the one we are a savour

------ ---- ----- ---

The apostle was the most eminent minister of the Gosp. He preached in many places Above all others the most Remarkable for his success but yet his success was not alike in all places amongst all sorts of People nor with different persons in the same places and among the same sort of People success Least where had been Great advantage Long Enjoyed

________________________

The apostle in the verses immediately preceeding the v text

___________

The meaning of the words is this we are unto G. a sweet savour of X in them that are saved and in them that perish. i. e. the work that we do in preaching the Gospel [^009-note1] whether to it be effectual or no is acceptable to God our Labour is if we are faithfull our Labour is an accept an acceptable offering to G. in a sw & he smells a sweet savour in our offering we are a sweet savour of X X wnt in X G. is well pleased in J.
