### 574. Matt. 13:3-4(a)

### 574.Matt. 13'3-4 (11_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

________________________________________________________________________

Matt. 13: 3, 4. *Behold a sower went forth to sow and when he sowed some seeds fell by the way side and the fowels**of the air**came and devoured them up* .

________________________________________________________________________

There are scarce any parts of the Holy SS. that are fuller of instruction than Christ's parables . There are these things to be observed concerning the instruction that is given in them that there there the instructions given are more Comprehensive very Comprehensive there is much more Contained in those similitudes that are used in these parables than could be plainly & particularly expressed in without a similitude & then when the simi litude is understood that Instruction is held forth with Greater Light for the openness of the similitude greatly illustrates the truth taught & not only illustrates but Confirms the similitudes used dont only shew the nature of things but the Reason of them & then the Instruction given in these parables is given in such a way as does especially tend to help the me engage the attention & help the memory and tis observable that X in his Instructions does [^010-note1] Insist on the most important contained in his Parables does insist on the most Important things of Relig. that do most nearly concern our Et. salvation. The words that have been now Read you all Know that is the beginning of [-] the Parable of the sower & the seed which you are all well acquainted with . . I propose by the help of G. to raise several observations from this Parable & make some Improvemt of Each one of them.

________________________________________________________________________

OBS. [ ] Those that G. sends forth to Preach the Gosp. may fitly be compared to Husbandmen going forth to sow seed their seed . X has probably a special Respect to hims. in this Parable who is the L. of the Harvest the owner of the field. He went forth unto all parts of the Land of Canaan sowi preaching the Gospel of the Kingd. & all sorts multitudes were want to Resort to him. he was continually preaching the Gosp. of the K. from town [--] to Town & wherever he went the multitude Thronged him and he preached the word to all sorts of Persons [^010-note2]

_____________________

But X has [-] Respect only to hims. but to others that are sent by him that Come to preach the word in his name . ministers of the Gosp. are the servants of the H L owner of the field that are sent forth to sow his seed. & when such comes to a people either as their being Regularly called to office among them as their settled pastor or is providentially sent among them they ought to be Looked upon as sent by the owner of the field of the Θ to sow his seed.

______________________

when a sower Goes forth to sow there are three things . there is the Husbandman & the seed that he bears & the Ground in which it is sowen . & his work which is to sow.

________________

1. There is the Husbandman. [ ] The work of ministers of the Gosp. is often in SS. compared to the work of an husbandman sometimes they are called Labourers in the vineyard sometimes Labourers in God sometimes Labourers in Gods vineyard. ministers are not called to be Idle but to Labour the work Busi the Husbandman Calling is a Labourious calling . he eats his bread with the sweat of his face & so it is [^010-note3] with faithfull ministers & so is the business of a faithfull minister that tis a business of Great & continual in a husbandman is required not only hard Labour but Constant care or continual oversight of his fields & the state of his husbandry or watchfullness over & also a Great deal of Prudence in the manage ment of his business improving the Proper season for plowing sowing Reaping &c---- & so it is also with the work of a Gosp. minister . The End of the Labours of the husbandman in his

___________________________________

2. There is the seed that they bear which is the word of G. seed is

__________________________________________

2. There is the seed that they bear which is the word of G. sometimes ministers of the Gospel are Compared to a vessels that contain a precious tres treasures as 2. Cor. 4. 7. they are Earthen vessels in which God sends a pre & offers [ ] inesti mable treasures to the Chil. of men This Treasure is the word of G. the Glorious Gospel of X Contains many Glorious doc. excellent counsels Gracious calls & inclinations & precious promises which are all as so many Jewels precious Jewels . but in this parable the [^010-note4] word of G. that they are sent forth with is compared to seed that they go forth with this is that precious seed spoken of Ps. 126. 7.

____________________

The word of G. fitly compared to seed in the Heart as its end is bringing forth fruit and this fruit is not brought forth unless it be any otherwise than by taking Root in abiding in the Ground in which it is sowen & taking Root there . taking being well Received making imp deep impression being understood taking hold of the Affections having a durable Effect in the temper & dispo sition of the soul & it is Like seed in this Respect that seed sowen in the Ground fin ally produces perfect fruit the first thing is the seed begins to be warmed & quickened by the beams of the sun then & to be expanded by the Rain being moistened by the Rain & then it begins a little to put forth sprouts & these Grow at first under Ground then it app as it Grows bigger it appears Plainly above Ground & so by the Influences of the beams of the sun & the benefit of the Rain it gradually grows more & more puts forth Leaves appears [^010-note5] more & more Conspicuous & beautifull Grows taller & taller & then the tender fruit is put forth till at Length it is ripened & then Gathered [ ] in.

_________________

so it is with the seed of the word. it is first quickened by the Influences blessed beams & Pourd Influences of the son of Righ. & by the sp. of G. as the dew & Rain of Heaven & pourd out in the soul like a sp. shower in the dry & thirsty Ground then the seed begins to put forth roots & sprouts & oftentimes at first Conversion Grace is very small Like the sprouts in a seed when it first begins to sprout & Grace when it is small is difficultly seen & Leaves persons in doubt whether they have any grace or no . as like the small sprouts of the seed in th when first quickened they bent big enough to get above Ground & so they are not seen but Grace in the Hearts of the saints Grows more & more & the New Creature grows fuller & taller & at Length is very Conspicuous & plain to be seen & the fruit Ripens & more & more [^010-note6] till it Comes tois Gathered into the Xs barn.

_______________________________________________________________________

3. Here is the Ground in which the seed is sown viz the Hearts of m the hearers . the Hearts of men are fitly compared to the Earth . by reason

of [ ] & theref G. calling in men to hear his word says calls em the Earth . O Earth Earth . Earth -------------

they are fitly compared to the Earth because of mens meer original which is of the dust of the Ground . for we are made of the dust of the Earth dust we are & unto dust we must Return . the first man was of the Earth Earthy .

____________

& then this fitly Represents the state of the hearts of all men by nature they are Earthly . They are debased from their primi tive Excellency & sink down to the Earth are of Earthly dispositions are cold & [ ] stony & lifeless like the Earth & naturally inclined to bring forth briars & thorns & evil use an useless & Evil Growth as the Earth will do without t Earth is . The Earth without tillage will [^010-note7] be dreadfully overrun with all every manner of thing that is unprofitable & detrimental in a field so will the soul of man if neglected & Let alone

______________________

4. Here is the work of the husbandman which which bears the seed which is to sow the seed in that Ground . the work of a minister of the Gosp in Preaching the word is very fitly compared to sowing of seed he delivers that precious word of G. that with G. which which he brings un to the People he is sent to as the Hus bandman scatters the seed forth out of his hand . a faithfull minister is Care full to give every one his portion of meat & to pre accomodate his Instructions & Ex O Exhortations to all sorts of persons persons in all Circumstances as an Hus the Husbandman endeavours to scatter his seed Equally all over his field

____________

He that sows the seed is not the author of the fruit that the field brings forth all that he Can do is but to cast forth the seed out of his [--] hand but he is not the au [^010-note8] thor of the Effect there is of the seed. when he has sown the seed he must leave it & tis not he but the sun & the Rain & that Cherishes & Enlivens the seed & Causes it to put forth Roots & sprouts & afterwords to Grow & Come to perfection . all this is what the husbandman has no hand in it is not he that quickens the seed & makes it Grow so far forth that he dont so much as Know it is done so it is with the preachers of the Gospel. mark 4. 26. 27. 28. -------------------------------------

-----------------

------- the h ministers Can but deliver their message ---- --------

_______________________________________________________________________

IMPROVEMT 1 Hence ministers should be faithfull in there work & should Leave the Event with G. as the Husband he should do as the prudent husbandman who is carefull to observe the proper season of sowing his seed & is carefull to sow the seed well to scatter it equally in his field & then to Leave it to the sun & [^010-note9] Rain & Influences of the Heaven to make it spring & Grow & Carry it to Perfection . so ministers must do their work & b & be prudent & diligent in it should Labour to observe the Circum stances of his hearers & to watch for the most opportune & advantageous seasons of fo setting home such & such Exhorations warnings & counsels & to give every one his portion of meat & this he must not forbear to do whatever discouragemt he Labours & however dark things appear he when he has done his duty he must Leave the Los Rest with G. & as most such . The Husbandman cant command the Rain or the sun he may sometimes sow the seed the & the seed may rot in the Ground with the Cold for want of the warm influences of the sun to Cherish it as he may p as the produce of the field may Perish through drought & for want of the [ ] dewes & showers of Heaven upon it but yet the husbandman if he be unsuccessfull sometimes for want of [^010-note10] his seeds being thus blessed prospered yet [-] wont Leave off his husbanding but will still continue to sow his field at the Return of the proper season . the dis a minister must deliv. the message tho with which G. has sent him whether they b h they he will hear or whether they will forbear he they are called to be fishers of men and tho it may sometimes be[-] so that they may toil all night & Catch mothing may Let down the net & draw it up again time & empty time after time yet at Xs Command they must still Let down the net . & in the way of their duty must we & tho it may seem Long before they have a harvest yet they must do as the Husbandman does after he has sown his [-] seed he waits paitently for the Harvest till Gods time Comes Jam. 5. 7. behold the Husbandman waiteth for the precious fruit of the Earth & hath Long patience for it until he receives the Early & the Latter rain. 2. Hence a People that hear the word should [^010-note11] earnestly pray for the Warm influences of the sun of Righ. & the sp. & & for sp. showers to succes succeed it

____________________________________

2. what has been said holds forth that which ought to matter of Humbling to us as it shows us what teaches what our Hearts are by nature They are like the Earth . they we have our souls at first were noble & Excellent they shone in the Image of G. & then they were full of Light & heat & love & were like the stars of Heaven the soul of man then was like a star of Heaven Glittering in its brightness . our Hearts then were full of an Holy & divine savour & a divine flame but we have lost all that heat & brightness our hea souls are dreadfully debased and have become the vile Gr Ear dirt of the Earth having nothing in them of any Heavenly Light or warmth but are cold or Glory what is esteemed [-] more vile than the Ground . but the vile dirt the dark cold stony Ground is not more destitute of Life Heat & Light in it self than our hearts naturally are . they are are altogether dead. [ ] senseless of sp. [^010-note12] things [ ] wholly in unfit for spiritual & vital acts . wholly dark . any wholly without any form or Comliness . such are we by nature . what Reason theref have we to Abhor in our selves how unbecoming is it of th a sight is it to see the dust of the Earth lifting up it self in pride extolling it self as tho it were something very worthy & precious & selfsuffi cient .

______________________________________________________________________

3. Hence a People that Hear the Word of preached should Earnestly pray for the warm Influences of the son of Righ. & sp. showers to prosper & succeed it It was evident the manner of the Heathen to worship the sun & this Kind of Idolatry is supposed to be the first Idolatry that ever was in the Θ . they worshipped the sun because they found they very much depended upon it they saw that all their tillage their plowing & sewing was in vain without its Influ ences they saw that it was the benign Influ ences of the sun that caused their seed to sprout & the Corn to Grown & that brought it to perfection . that [-] was an unreasonable [^010-note13] Kind of worship . because the sun material sun is but an a Creature of G. & an Instrument in his hand to Cherish the fruits of the Earth & Cause em to Grow. But we ought to worship the s. of Righ. for on that we absolutely depend that sun is indeed the hightest fountain of Light & heat & the fountain of Life in vain are all means & all Instruments without it . we should when the seed is sown we should Look up to this sp. sun & beg its blessed Influences to Cherish Quicken it to cause it to send forth for otherwise it will as it were rot in the Ground.

________________________

Hither ministers should have their Eye they are in SS are called burning & shining Lights but they have neither Light nor heat any further than as they derive it from the sun of Righ. & Can un C Communicate no Light nor Life nor fruitfullness to their hearers any further than [ ] they are made use of as Glasses to Convey & Reflect the beams the of the Light of the Θ [^010-note14]

______________________

Hither it is that a people should Look when they have heard the word preached especially after they have heard it powerfully preached after G. has sent an [-] messinger with extraordin ary fervency to deliv. his Message to them

_______________

a People in such a Case should Cry Earnestly to that Glo. sun. who is the brightness of Gods Glory & the express Image of his person. who is full of [-] Light & divine [-] heat . in whom dwells all the fuln. of the Godnead bodily & is more full of sp. Light & of Grace than the sun is of light. We see that the sun tho it has been shining for many ages has every day & Every moment been pouring forth of its light & warm beams upon all the Θ yet [--] the fountain is not Exhausted there is enough still for ought appears there is as much Light & heat in the sun now as there was the first day it was made & such is its fullness that there is Enough for all our fields there are thous. & millions of fields that are made fruitfull [^010-note15] by it & because One field receives so much there is never the Less for another this is some fu Image of the fullness of X where there is Power & Light & Grace & Love enough for all . which should Encourage us to Look up to him & pray for that he would pour send down of his blessed Influences & [-] Cherish the seed that is sowen in [-] our Hearts & make it to Grow & become fruitfull there.

______________________________________________________________________

we often pray for Rain because to water the seed that we sow in our fields because we Know that our sowing will be in vain without if there bent showers of Rain upon our fields all the seed sowen there will come to nothing . How much should we pray for Heavenly showers to water the seed of the word sowen in Our Hearts is it not of Greater Importance that our Hearts should be fruitfull than our fields shall we Lament it when we see our fields [^010-note16] barren & shall be we [be] Easy when we carry about with us barren Hearts Hearts that bring forth nothing but briars & thorns & [ ] [-] an unprofitable & noxious Growth fit for nothing but to be burnt

____________________

when Rain is witheld in the summer time but for a few weeks what and the produce of our seed begins to Languish & wither the what di concern are we in how do we will for rain & Keep observing every Cloud that rises . hoping that we shall have some rain from it & we are forward in such a Cause to set apart some time to pray to G. for Rain because [-] we see that if we have none we are like to Go Hungry & we shall pinched for want of the Comforts of this Life and shall we be easy when we see that sp. showers are witheld not only for some weeks but year after year shall we be grind sensibly Grievd & concerned when we see the Leaves of the corn wither turn yellow & wither up . & shall we not [^010-note17] be grievd when we see our [ ] souls [-] [-] perishing & Languishing & appearing as tho if they are almost dead when you go forth into the fields at the time of Harvest & find your crops very thin & Light & bring but a little Home . with what grief in of Heart do you bring home your few blasted sheaves & shall [-] it with no Great Concern that you see littlevery little fruit of the word brought forth in your lives & Converstaions Hearts & lives if it should be be so at any time that your fields have brought forth so little that you have enough for Your families & at the End of the year you Know not which way you shall have your Chil provided for & [ ] Kept from starving would not this be a Great Exercise [?] to you would not the bowels of Parents yearn towards their Children in such a Case & [-] yet [ ] you that are parents see th the souls of your Chil in a [ ] Famishing Languishing [^010-note18] condition & ready to sink into Eternal perdition for want of sp. showers & yet be easy without those spiritual showers. Let the Consideration of these things set us up in the up in this place earnestly to look to G. for those Heavenly Influences of his sp. that we need to Give [-] success to the preaching of the word. Go forth as a servant that G. has

sent forth to How Lamentable have been our Circum stances of Late have been very Lamentable the Circumstances of them that Look upon thems to be Godly [-] here many of them yea most of them seem very Lamentable dead & cold & dry Like that dust of the Earth their Hearts have had very little of the showers of Heaven very little of the warmth of the sun of Righ. in them when we Consid. how what profession they have Made tis a most Lamentable thing to think of it tis Lamentable for them & Lamentable for the Interest of Relig . wounding to its Reputation & wounding to & Ruinous to [^010-note19] the souls of others

we have have I have shown you from the foregoing verse we have already shown who considered one of those sorts of Ground that the seed of the that the saviour sowed fell upon viz the way side & what became of the seed that fell there

we are I am now to the second sort of Ground men tioned in the parable & the Consequence of w con which is spoken of in the two verses that have been now Read . & theref in these words may be observed . 1. the description of the Ground tis Called stony Ground where there is not much Earth or stony Places . where there is not much Earth . . i. e. the Ground was & nothing but a Rock excepting a thin covering of Earth which is one degree beyond the former sort of Ground that the seed fell upon viz the way side which was hard throughout both above & beneath had like a Path that had been trodden upon so Long that [^012-note1] the very suf surface was become exceeding hard the But the sort of Ground spoken of here and But the Ground spoken of here is a little dif ferent tis as hard as that but the hardness is not on top but underneath there is a thin Covering or sprinkling of Earth here upon the Rock so that Just at the top of the Ground it is not so hard tho underneath it is as ha a meer Rock .

The doc. of the Gosp. about X & free Grace through him . about Xs satisfac tion & Righ. about mens Emptiness & X fulln . are delightfull Glo . doc. in his Eye & this is the Reason that he has Joy in hearing them Preached . we observed that three things in what is said of the seed sowen in that fell on the sort of Ground that is spoken of in is here spoken of 1. That the Ground is described 2. How the seed Grows that is sowen in this sort of Ground . 3. what becomes of that Growth at Last .

we have already insisted on the description of the Ground in the former part of the 5. v. I now proceed to consider the Growth of the seed sowen in this Ground . [5 lines blank] Prop . sudden Conversions are very often false P Prop. oftentimes false conversions are very sudden ones . It is particularly Remarked in the text that the seed that was sowen in stony places . sprung up forthwith . . & then the Rea son is given viz. that they had no deepness of Earth which implies as much as that the seed when it has not dee it is a more Common thing for seed to spring up very suddenly when it [^013-note1] has not deepness of E. than when it has . seed that is sowen upon a Rock where there is only a thin covering or sprinkling of Earth . cant sink in for for it must lie very neer the surface of the Ground & theref it Cant be a Growing Long before it will it will come up quick .

Those that suddenl are suddenly Comforted after first awakenings hant had time to Learn by Exp. what they are . Observa Those who are Religious only from super ficial Impressions made on their Hearts

___________________________

Observa That Relig. that arises only from superficial Impressions made in the is wont to wither when the away when it comes to be tried by the difficulties of Religion .

I. The Persons that are only sup have only superficial Impressions do oftentimes appear to be Religious sup The seed sowen upon the Rock wa stony Ground made some impression it sank in a little way & there was some Effect there was a Growth from the seed . & the Growth for what appears was at first as fair as that which was in Good Ground or the Lamps of the foolish virgins at first for wh ought that in s appears burnt as bright for a while as one the Lamps of the wise vir gins . Those whose Hearts Remain a Re hard as a Rock from superficial Impressions made [^014-note1] by the word . may yet for a while continue re be Reformed they may live morally may avoid those wicked practices that they formerly used to Indulge thems. in & may be carefull of their behaviour amongst men . They may make a fair Profession and put on a Religious Countenance may appear serious & devout & may Join with the People of G. may seem to be desire their Company as Judas was a Great while a Companion with the other discip. & as ab Achitophel was a Companion with david . see Ps. 55. 13. 14. ------------------------

-----------------------------------

& may seem to be much Engaged [--] for a while in Religious duties may be Constant in secret prayer & & diligent & strict in attending on ordinances forward for Relig. dis talk & frequent in private Relig. meetings & may show a zeal against sin & be seem zealous for Promoting Relig . Prop II The Relig. of such as are only superficially [^014-note2] Impressed is but temporary . The Chil. of Israel had their Hearts impressed in the wilderness . they were Greatly affected at the Red sea & were Relig. at first they sang Gods Praise but their Reli this Relig. did not hold for they soon forgot his works . Ps. 78. 57 . G. Complains of Israel that their Goodness was as the morning Cloud . --------- Hosea . 6. 4 . The Lamps of the foolish virgins burnt for a while the w they Complain that when the bridegroom Comes that their Lamps were gone out It was a true Proverb that we have in Prov. 26. 11 that the dog will Return to his vomit & --------------- many of G X disciples that were affected with his preaching & followed him a while yet afterwards went Back & walked no more with him & so the Apostles tell us of some in those days [^014-note3] that Joined with Xtians a while but then went out from em because they were not of them Tis often mentioned as the distinguishing Character of the Characteristick of the truly Godly that they should hold on their way & that they should Continue in Xs word & to the End tis mentioned as a mark of Xs true servants that he should find em faith fully doing their duty when he come Blessed are those servants when his L

III. The difficulties that attend Relig. are the proper There are many difficulties that attend Religion a being thoroughly & Perseveringly Religious . The Christian Relig . is not a thing Calculated to suit sute mans carnal inclinations but is Exceeding There Indeed there may be somethings in Relig. that may very well agree with mans natural Inclinations Especially in a Xtian Countrey & among a People where Relig is in Cre much in Credit there is very But Every where a being universally & Perseveringly Relig. wa is a thing attended with many difficulties Hence we Read of a narrow way that Leads to Life [^014-note4] X was abundant in signifying to his disciples the difficulties followers the difficulties of difficulties that would attend being his d. many of them expected Great Θly Prosperity by it being carried way away with the notion of a tem the messiahs temporal Kingd. but X Let them Know that it was Riches & Θly honours & Pleasures that were to be expected in following him but trouble & Contempt & a bearing the and bearing the Cross he told them that the through much tribulation they should Enter into the K. of Heaven that he did not Come to find Peace on Earth but a sword & that through much tribulation they must Enter into the K. of H.

& there are some duties that are Easy to some that are very difficult to others by Reason of their particular Constitutions & natural Tempers or the peculiar particular Circumstances if men might have the Liberty of picking & Choosing among the [^014-note5] Commands yea if there were only that Liberty given that every man might have the Liberty of setting do aside some one particular Command . Relig woul it would be [-] an abundantly Easier thing for men to be Xtian than it is now . But there is no such Liberty given . Let mens natural dispositions or their Circumstances it the be what they will every Command must be Obeyed .

The difficulties that attend Relig. are fitly in the Text compared to the scorching heat of the sun The sun dont alwaies shine burning hot The beams of the sun are not alwaies burning hot th when the seed that we sow in our fields first sprin begins to Grow in the spring the weather is temperate & the sun beams are very moderate . but if ever that seed brings any fruit to Perfection that Growth Continues a while the sun will Get up higher & then it will shine down upon with a vehement heat all our seed must stand first & last stand the burning heat of the sun.

IV Prop. The difficulties that attend Relig. are the Proper trial of sincerity . the scorching heat of the sun was the proper trial of the Growth from the seed sowen whether it had good Rooting or no & this is the very trial by which it that without root ing is manifested & distinguished from that which had none . so the blowing of the wind is the Proper trial of the weight of what is upon the Threshing floor to distinguish & separate wheat from Chaff . The [ ] wheat the Grain is solid & heavy & the will bear the blowing of the wind [---] when it is winnowed but the Chaff is Light & has no substance & theref the wind will carry it away . the difficulties of Relig. are to Professours of Relig as the wind is to the Wheat & Chaff . & there for those troubles are [-] called Xs fan by which he Purges his floor . thus John [^014-note9] the Bap. tells the people . Math . 3. 11. 12 He that cometh after me is mightier -------- ------ whose fan is in his hand & he shall ---- ------- one thing that John has Respect to here is doubtless those Great troubles & that should arise after Xs Resurrection which should try mens sincerity . and those difficulties that attend Relig. are doubtless also one thing intended by X in the Latter end of the 7. Chap. of Math when he speaks there of the Rain descending & the winds blowing & floods coming to whereby the House is the House is tried as to the strength of its founda . whether it be built on s a Rock or on the sand if an house be built on never so weak a founda. yet if there be never any wind nor Rain nor flood to come nigh it to de nor any thing to bear [-] hard upon it the weakness of the founda . never will be discovered the House will stand as well as if it were upon a Rock . a Rock but storms & floods are the proper trial of the foundation to prove whether it be a Rock or sand sand bank .

The diff. between [--] the Relig. of Hyp. & tru the sincere Relig. of true saints is Represented by this in Rev. 3. 17. 18 the hypocrite thinks he is rich & increased with but his Riches is not true Gold but the saint has gold tried in the fire that which stand the fire & theref is evidently true Gold Thou sayest I am rich & increased with Goods says X & Knowest not that thou art ------- ------------ I counsel thou says he to pur cha buy of me Gold tried in the fire that thou mayest be rich . we have already Insisted [-] upon what is said in this Parable [-] of the two two sorts of Ground on which the seed fell viz the way side & the stony Ground . and in the text may be observed two things things 1. The quality of the Ground. it was thorny Ground . not that the thorns were al ready grown up but it was Ground that was full of the seeds and roots of thorns when it is said some fell among thorns it is as much as to say they fell upon Ground that uncultivated Ground Ground that never had been plowed and fitted for the seed The If thorns sp Thorns are the spontaneous growth of the ground that Come up of thems. in land that is not tilled and it seems to have been especially so in that Countrey that Land that Lay fallow & was not plowed would of it self be quickly overrun with briars & thorns & theref it is said in the 4. Chap of Jer. 3. v. Break up your fallow Ground & sow not among thorns . which seems to Imply as much as that Ground that was Lay fallow was not plowed was [ ] wont to be overgrown with them thorns and theref when it is said in the text [^015-note1] that some of the seed fell among thorns it is as much as to say that some fell on uncultivated unplowed Land .

## 579a. Matt. 22:9-10 579a. Matt. 29'10

Jonathan Edwards

### 579a. Matt. 29'10

1. Children.You are all of you naturally the children of God’s wrath. If you are not converted, God is angry with you ever day; if you should die in the condition you are now in, you would surely go to hell... Many children die, and all those of them that are not converted, go down to the pit of hell into everlasting burnings, to dwell there forever and ever. So that you must be converted and get and interest in Christ. You cannot bear hell, there is none of you can bear it; you could no bear to lie in such a fire as is made every day upon the hearth for one quarter of an hour; how then will you bear to lie in the dreadfull fire of forever and ever? For those that are cast into that fire never shall be delivered out of it...

You do not know but that you may die now, while you are children; it may be, there is but a very little while before the time will come when you must die; for ought we know the threatening distemper may in a little time come into the town, and carry off multitudes of children, as it has lately done in many other towns. There are some towns where the greater part of the families in the whole town have died with that distemper, so that though the town before was full of children, yet in a little time, but few have been left. In some families where there has been a considerable number of children, all the children have died with that distemper. We have had but little of that distemper in this town hitherto, but we do not know that our turn to have it is at hand; for ought we know, in a little time it may come and make as dreadful work here, as it has in many other towns...

If you are converted, you need not be afraid to die, you need not be afraid of the threatening distemper. Let it come when it will, it will not do you any hurt: no distemper, no death, can do them any hurt who have an interest in Christ... How will you bear hereafter to see other children shining in robes of glory, standing at the right hand of Christ, and rejoicing in the love of Christ, while you stand a poor, wicked, miserable hateful, creature, among devils, going to be cast into hell along with them forever and ever?

2. The Youth.The devil has had your tender age; your childhood has been spent in his drudgery, and you have been learning of him all the days of your life, ever since you were infants and have been capable of learning anything... You were foolish and perverse like wild asses' colts, and you acted like a child of the devil...

Let the time past of your life suffice that you have served your lusts, and served Satan. Now give up all the remaining part of your life to an earnest striving in religion. Do not think now for a little while to be serious and religious, and by and by to turn again to frolicking and vain mirth. If there be no hurt in frolicking, and it has no tendency to any hurt, and if it is in no respect contrary to the increase of religion in the soul,-then frolick now. Why do you leave it off? Why should not that which is harmless, and which in no way hinders the good of the soul, be as much in fashion in a time of awakening and religious concern as at any other time? The best way is to be consistent with yourself...Therefore do not avoid this practice now under a notion of its hindering your soul's good, and by and by change your tone and say there is no hurt in it, and plead that there is time for all things... If it be a thing of no ill tendency, then there is no sin in it at all. Go on frolicking in a time of awakening, keep on in this way, and see if ever you come to good in this way. Be consistent with yourself one way or the other; either always practice it or else always avoid it. Do not sometimes avoid it, as if you yourself were convicted in your own conscience that it was a thing of ill tendency, and then at the same time plead that there is no manner of hurt in it.

3. The middle-aged.Thus your best is gone; you have it not now to offer to God, if you had never so great a mind to it. It has all been spent and wasted in the service of the devil... The hardness of persons' hearts grows as fast as their age; as they grow older and older in a Christian state, their condition grows more and more dreadful...

Instead of your seasons becoming more convenient, have not you found on the contrary that they have grown more and more inconvenient? Do not you find now that you are much more incumbered with the business and concerns of the world than when you were young, your heart more and more charged with those things, and less and less at liberty, and less and less disposed to mind the affairs of your soul?... Is it not now high time to set about the work, without any more putting off? When you have been pursuing a shadow thirty or forty years together without overtaking it, is it not then high time to give over?...

What times have you seen and passed through, what seasons of remarkable mercy of the Spirit of God! You have seen several such seasons, wherein many have been brought in; there has been a harvest of souls for Christ, but you have always been as the barren heath in the wilderness. Others have had their eyes opened, but you have remained blind. Others have flourished like the palm tree, but you have remained like a dead dry tree among them. In the late, remarkable, and wonderful time of awakening, six years ago, when Christ so gloriously appeared and rode forth in the chariot of salvation, and so many had a new song put into their mouths, you have no part of lot in the matter. And since that, God has wrought great things in other countries, in England and in Germany, in Scotland and Wales, and in Pennsylvania and New York and the Jerseys, in Carolina and Georgia; but still your soul has remained dead. And now lately God has revived His work here again, and has revived the work of conversion amongst us, and some have been brought home to Christ; but still you are left behind. And will you still delay, will you let slip this opportunity also, as you have all others that you have had? consider how swiftly you are going down the hill of life; the state of your soul does not remain at a stand, five or six years make a great alteration. As for instance, you are now under far greater disadvantage, since this last time of the outpouring of the Spirit of God, your heart is very much hardened, and your guilt vastly increased since that time, and your advantage far less than it was then. But yet Christ invites you now, and now there is a blessed opportunity for all that are alive; and though the opportunity you now have is not so great as you have had heretofore, yet it is greater in all probability than you ever will have again.

4. The aged.If there are any here who are come to old age, and yet are in A Christless condition, I would now call on them, though it be at the eleventh hour...You have nothing left but your withered old age to offer to God, a few days in the decay and latter end of your life...

Indeed, to behold one in your circumstances, an old grey headed man or woman going about stooping with age, and yet in a Christless condition, is one of the most awful sights in the world. Your soul is every whit as precious as the souls of young persons, you are under as much necessity of salvation as they, and in some respects under much greater necessity; for if you erish, you will have a vastly more dreadful damnation than those that die in youth, for you have spent all your life in nothing but sin...

You have lived under a former minister, and though he lived long, yet your love to your idols and your unbelief, blindness, and obstinacy in rejecting Christ, have outlived him, and live to this day... The whole life of man is given to prepare for eternity, and all is short enough; but you have spent most of it, and are ten times further from a preparation for eternity than when you were a little child. Instead of preparing for heaven, you have all your days been preparing for hell.. You have as it were gone through the world, and made thorough proof of the service of sin, and now look back, and consider what fruit you have had of all, what have you got by it?

Classification by sex:*1. The men.*

Possibly the male sex, by reason of their superior strength and influence, are more exposed to a rough ness of spirit, less easily affected and subdued, more ready to stand out against what is affecting and awakening; and so your superior place and your superior power may be a snare to you, and probably are the undoing of the male sex, and are one reason why there commonly are so many more converted of the female sex than of the male. Also the circumstances of the affairs and business of the male sex does in some respects expose them to many more temptations than those of the female. Their business leads them more into the world, to be concerned abroad with more persons and to be concerned more extensively with the affairs and business of the world, when they have more worldly objects in their view to tempt them. They are in some respects more earthly, and it is more difficult for them to keep unspotted from the world, and you are in some respects more in the way of the snares of the god of this world, and particularly are more in the way of publick contentions and broils, wherein men are wont to divide into parties and set up their wills and envy one another and hate one another and plot against one another... Doubtless it is the undoing of many men that they were born of that sex: the business in which they by that means have been concerned and the temptation to which they have been exposed have proved their ruin... And of this you may be assured, that the greater your strength is and larger your sphere of action, the more God requires and expects of you; and if you continue I a way of wickedness, the greater will your guilt be and the more dreadful your condemnation at last...

And as the me of this town have long been divided into two parties, I would now earnestly call upon both parties to improve the present season to seek the good of their souls. Let who will be right in those affairs about which you have been so divided and in which your sprits have been so often heated and ruffled, and in the management of which has been so much grudging and backbiting one another; let who will be chiefly in fault. Christ this day invites you both... If you of both parties will hearken to the call and invitation of Christ this day, and will throw by all those contentions with which your minds have been so much taken up, and will apply yourselves with one mind to seek and serve Christ and make your calling and election sure, it is to be hoped that both of your will hereafter meet together in heaven and there dwell forever in everlasting peace and love. There are no parties, no secret jealousies, one of another, no gathering together in distinct companies to judge and backbite others, no plotting and contriving one against another; but there you shall be united in the same interest, and shall be of one mind and of one heart and one soul forever.

*2. The Women.*

Though in the fall of man, the female sex falls under peculliar dishonour in that woman first eat of the forbidden fruit and tempted the man, yet this sex has peculiar honour in the affair of the redemption of the second,... and all you, if you are converted, will become mothers of Christ in a more honourable and blessed sense than the virgin Mary was, for it is more blessed to have Christ conceived in the heart than in the womb.

As the male sex has some particular disadvantages, so also has the female. Through their weakness they are in some respects more easily prevailed upon by the temptations of the devil. The devil, knowing this, craftily began with Eve when he attempted to seduce man from his first integrity. You have therefore the more need of watchfulness and care, lest as the serpent beguiled Eve through his subtilety, so he should seduce and ruine you.

Let young women this day harken to the invitation of Christ. If you will do so, this glorious person will be your lover, yea, he will be your glorious bridegroom. You are invited this day to the marriage feast of the king’s son, not only as a guest, but as a bride. He is your suitor, he courts you; importunately he stands at your door and knocks, saying, "Open to me, my sister, my spouse, my head is wet with the drops of the night." And let me, in Christ’s stead, beseech you to give him your hearts; let him have your love who is fairer than the sons of men and is the most excellent, lovely, and honourable lover; come to him and spend your youth, yea, your whole life, in his love, in communion with him. Yield yourselves to Christ and he will adorn you with excellent ornaments. Your adorning shall not be that outward adorning of the wearing of gold and putting on fine and rich apparel, but it shall be the ornament of a meek and quiet spirit that is in the sight of God of great price.

Let those women that have the care of families this day harken to the call of Christ. Many of you may be ready to think that your disadvantages are peculiar by reason of the care and cumbrance you have in your families, and the many hindrances you have from religious duties. And doubtless you have many temptations and difficulties which may justly make you lament and bewail the loss of past opportunities when you were young. But yet if you have a heart engaged to do the utmost that lies in your power, according to your opportunities and as your circumstances and necessary business of your families will allow, if you make the concern of your soul your great concern and make all temporal affairs give place to it, and attend to the outward concerns of your families in no other way than that to which the duty of your place obliges you, and improve what advantages and opportunities remain to you, to your utmost, and continue so to do, begging of God His help, I say, if you do thus, there is yet hope that you may obtain mercy.

Classification by class:*1. Those of low rank.*

However you may be had in but little esteem, may be despised and slighted and accounted of no great consequence in the world, and have not that respect shewn to you that many of your neighbours have, yet the great king sends forth his messengers to invite you to come... In the high ways we shall meet many that are very low in the world, many poor and despised. Christ sends his ministers to gather up such, and such as being poor vagrants sleep under hedges like the beasts as having no house of their own, and be so despised that none will take them in.

Christ himself, when he was upon earth, confined himself to your condition. He did not appear in the world in the circumstances of a man of note, but in the state of the poor and despised. He was of low parentage: his mother was a young woman of low degree, her husband Joseph was not one of their men of influence, but an obscure person...

The great God has a greater respect for one saint, though mean and despised among men, than He has for the greatest wicked monarch on the earth, and will show a thousand times greater regard to him in what He will do for him. The saints are His jewels, but the wicked men, however rich, learned, and great, are of no account with Him...

But if you refuse and will neglect your salvation, then you will not only be low and despised in this world, but so will you be forever; you will not only be despised of men, but you will forever be despised of God also. You will not only continue to be lightly esteemed as you now are, but you shall be thrust down into an immensely greater degree of contempt...

1. So you that are weak in understanding and are comparatively ignorant, and are upon that account no much accounted of, you see that your judgment of things is not much regarded, what you say in conversation is not so much taken notice of, your voice is not so much heard as others on publick affairs. Hearken this day to the call of Christ, and set yourself with all your might to seek and follow him. If you will give up and do so, Christ will make no objection of your weakness and your ignorance; you shall be as freely and as readily accepted as if you excelled in human learning and wisdom. Christ does as much invite you as any of the politicians, philosophers, or divines of the world. Christ is able savingly to enlighten you; he does not stand in need of great abilities or acquired knowledge in order to his imparting that knowledge that is saving; he has chosen the foolish things of the world to confound the wise, and the weak things of the world to confound the mighty, and the base things of the world, and things which are despised, hath God chosen, yea, and things which are not, to bring to nought things that are... The least beam of the light of the knowledge of the glory of God in the face of Jesus Christ is worth more than all the human knowledge that is taught in all the most famous colleges and universities in the world...

2. So you that are poor and have a much smaller part of the good things of this life than the greater part of your neighbours and are hard put to it to provide things necessary for yourself or family, and are often reduced to straits and difficulties that your neighbours about you, who have a greater fullness, know nothing of, hearken to the call of Christ this day, and look to him and follow him... If now you are mealy clad and forced to wear that which is coarse and homely, Christ has white raiment to clothe you... If you live upon mean fare and sometimes are pinched with hunger, come to Christ and you shall not have mean fare... And besides, if you mostly seek heavenly riches, this is the surest way for you to be provided for with those temporal good things that you need.

And when you have done with the world, when those things fail, you shall be received into everlasting habitations, where you shall hunger no more nor thirst anymore, but the lamb that is in the midst of the throne shall feed you...

But if you refuse to hearken to Christ and will neglect your salvation or seek it in but a poor, indifferent, unsteady manner, then as you are poor here, so you are like to be poor forever; here in this world you have little, and in another world you shall have nothing. How hard will be your lot: you will neither have a portion in this world nor in that which is to come; you will get neither heaven nor earth. While you stay here, you will go without those comforts and enjoyments of life that others have, suffering many hard things; and when you die, then you will be cast out to perish in everlasting famine... You will make but a hard shift to get through the world, and when you have finished your course, then you must go away into everlasting perdition. You are some of the meanest and lowest in this world, and many of the afflictions and hardships, and difficulties of the world fall to your share here; and when you have done with the world, then you must be thrust down infinitely lower, you shall be trodden down as the more of the streets. How much better then would it have been for you that you had never been born, than to be born to meet with so much difficulty and trouble in this life, and to have eternal misery in the life to come, and never, never to see any good?

3. So you that are servants and poor negroes. You are of those that are poor in the world, but harken to the call of Christ, and improve the present opportunity earnestly to seek your salvation... Though you are a servant, yet if you will come to Christ, and heartily give up your life to his service, you shall be the Lord’s freeman... If you refure to hearken to Christ, and live in the neglect of your salvation, then you will not only be the servant of men, but the servant of the devil, and will hereafter fall into his hands, and be in his possession forever.

*2. The middle class.*

I would apply myself to those that possess a greater measure of this world’s goods, and are in higher circumstances in the world.

If you are more regarded than some of your neighbours and have greater respect shewn you, if your words are looked upon as of greater weight than theirs, yet if you are in a natural state, you are contemptible in God’s eyes... If you have more land than some of your neighbours, a larger stock, and more comfortable and plentiful accommodations, yet none of those things can make you any other than a miserable creature if you are not of Christ... If you had the favour of God with your worldly enjoyments, they might well be sweet to you, you might eat your meal with gladness; but instead of that, you eat and drink under the wrath of God, you lie down and rise up under His wrath. When you look upon your buildings, your cattle, your stores that you have laid up, you may consider that brimstone is scattered upon them all.

When you sit down at a full table, yet there is God’s bow bent, His arrows ready, the dart pointing at your heart all the while you are satisfying your appetite. You eat and drink, and sleep and walk, and march over this part of hell, in slippery places. The ground you are upon is not solid ground, but is as it were hollow ground, and there is the dreadful pit of hell underneath. The covering is very thin and weak, men are continually dropping through into the pit, and lost irrecoverably; but one in many escapes, and you are in danger every moment.

Men are aprt to rely much on their worldly possessions and advantages, and to be much pleased to be themselves so much higher in the world than others, and to be greatly taken with it that they have so much more than they... Men’s worldly possessions and worldly honour with which they are so taken very commonly prove their undoing; setting their hearts so much upon them occasions them to neglect God, and so they have their portion in this life, and then they have enjoyed all that comfort that they have to enjoy in those things, they have ruined their soul’s salvation, there remains nothing else for them.

If you continue in a Christless condition, all that you have in the world will prove only to have fatted you for the slaughter, and ripened you for the wine press... And though now you sit forward in the meeting house, and have a higher seat than your poor, inferiour neighbours, yet hereafter you shall be set in a lower place in hell than those wicked men ho now sit behind you in the meeting house. Therefore now hearken to the calls brought you from God, do not let your worldly possessions be a weight to hinder you.

Therefore do not make excuse, and go away, one to his farm and another to his merchandize; but go and sell all that you have, and buy the pearl of great price... As to the world, you have already a considerable measure of it; but as to the good things of another, if you that are in a Christless condition have nothing of them, you have no part in the good of God’s chosen in the inheritance of Gods children. How many earthly things soever you have, you have not Christ, and so are aliens from the commonwealth of Israel, and strangers to the covenant of promise, having no hope, and are without God in the world, and therefore are miserably poor, and have nothing of your precious an immortal souls.

Now God is opening His grand treasure among us, His treasure of spiritual blessings; He has been letting open a fountain for the supply of the wants of poor souls. Therefore now improve your time, and put in for a share that you may not continue famishing, and perish in the midst of plenty.

Classification by moral condition:1. Those that have been guilty of immoral and wicked practices, against the light and convictions of conscience.

Are there not some here who have, with considerable deliberation and forethought of the sinfulness of an act, gone abreast against all that conscience could say?

Have not some of you thus been guilty of acts of uncleanness against the convictions of your consciences, has not there been some lascivious vile acts that you have been guilty of by yourself alone, actions not fit to be named, unfruitful works of darkness, or some lewd, immodest, lascivious behavior that you have been guilty of with some other person, contrary to the conviction of your own conscience? And so are there not some that have been guilty of gratifying of other lusts, as a grinding spirit after gain, or a spirit of pride, or a spirit of ill will against the conviction of your consciences?

2. Such sinners as have formerly been awakened and prompted to seek salvation and have been guilty of backsliding.

There are some such here; there are some here who were in a measure awakened in the last time of the pouring out of God’s spirit in this place, and sought salvation, who afterwards quenched the convictions of the spirit of God. It is an exceedingly great and dangerous sin of which you have been guilty, and very provoking to God... You have greatly wounded your own soul, so foolish have you been; when you had a precious opportunity in your hands, you would not improve it. If you had gone on earnestly seeking, in all probability you might before this time have been rejoicing with the gladness of God’s nation; but by quenching your conviction, you have had great blocks in the way to your salvation... Now it is a time of awakening amongst us, many are stirred up and are much affected; but how few, how very few of them, are those that were come to years of discretion in the last time of the outpouring of the spirit of God here, and were then awakened and lost their convictions. They looked back when flying out of Sodom, and there they stood like pillars of salt, or statues of stone, on the very spot where they were when they looked back; there they are still, they have not stirred out of the spot to this day. Warnings of fire and brimstone from God out of heaven do not move them any more, the flash of the lightnings, and the noise of the thunders in Sodom and Gomorrah do not move them at all. And how little do they seem to be moved by all that they now see of the world of God upon others?

But yet you must be converted: you cannot bear everlasting burnings; you are in absolute infinite necessity of conversion, and therefore you must improve what opportunity you have. It may be you will never be converted; but if ever you are, it is most likely to be in some such season as this, a time of the outpouring of the spirit, a time of gracious visitation... Therefore improve this opportunity, now rouse up yourself to lay hold on God;... as you have made your work more difficult, so double your diligence... But only take heed you do not lose this second opportunity, and that you do not quench convictions a second time.

3. Those who have been long seeking conversion, and have never yet obtained [it], that have been for many years uneasy about your being in a natural condition, and have been after a sort seeking to enter in at the strait gate...

You have had the spirit of God long striving for you, God has been nigh to you, and has long been calling, and knocking and waiting; and what a wretched use have you made of such an advantage?... And how often have you grieved the spirit of God by your slothfulness, and by your unsteadiness, and by your worldliness, and innumerable corrupt workings of heart and unsuitable unchristian thoughts, words, and deeds? How often have you been exhorted to be more in earnest, and how obstinate have you been in your slothfulness?... If you saw your behaviour since you have been in the wilderness in the true light, you would not think that God dealt hardly with you, that He has not yet comforted you; you would not think it a strange thing that others who have till of late been secure sinners are converted, and you left behind still... It is time to alter your course, and to leave off dreaming, and to wake thoroughly out of sleep, and apply yourself in real earnest to your great work; and not only to seek as you have done, but to strive to enter in at the strait gate, and to be violent for the kingdom of heaven.

4. All such as have lately been awakened and are now under awakenings.

To all such as do thus,... I now apply myself and would call upon them to improve the present opportunity... It may be, if you do not harken to those calls and strivings, they will prove the last knocks and calls that eer you will have of that naure; and if it should be so, then all external calls will forever be in vain... Commonly the time when a person is under the first convictions that ever he has is by far the best opportunity that ever he has to obtain salvation in his whole life; and if such an opportunity be diligently and thoroughly improved without backsliding, there is an exceedingly great probability of success.

5. Those living in a state of sinfulness and security. How brutishly selfish and stupid are you who have had so much to awaken you, and have seen such awakening providence of late, instances of sudden and surprising death, and have seen, and do now see, such works of an invisible power in the hearts of others, and hear so much of what is done in other parts of the world, and know that you are in a natural condition, and yet are secure in your sins?... If you think the wrath of God is not so terrible that persons need to be so much concerned to escape it, then go on and see how you will conflict with the wrath of an almighty God; see if your hand can be stronger, or your heart endure, in the day when God rises up and girds Himself with His omnipotence to glorify His justice and magnify His mighty power upon you, try and see what it is to fall into the hands of almighty God; see whether you can bear the devouring fire or dwell with everlasting burnings.

ConclusionSo let all hearken to the call of Christ, by his word, and in his providence, and by his spirit, this day: young men and maids, old men, middle aged, and little children, both male and female, both black and white, high and low, rich and poor together; great sinners, sinners against great light, against convictions of conscience, backsliders, old sinners and old seekers, self-rightesous murmurers, and quarrellers with God; those that are under convictions, and those that are senseless and secure, moral and vicious, good and bad, poor, maimed, halt, and blind, prodigals eating husks with swine, vagabonds and beggars in the highways and hedges, persons of every condition, and all parties, and every denomination whatsoever.

### 583. Rev. 14:3(a)

### 583.Rev. 14'3(11_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

__________________

In the five first verses of this Chap. we have an account of a vision that the apostle John had of the True Church that steadfastly adhered to Christ in the time of the Great and Generally Apostacy by wherein in the Θwandered after the beast & was Cor was Corrupted b with the abomin delusions & Idolatries of and the antiXtian Chh.

while the Rest of the Θ wandered followed the beast the beast with seven heads and ten horns those followed the Lamb. wheresoever he went

But the men souls of true believers are represented as chaste virgins that Kept thems. [^018-note1] pure for X alone to whom they were betrothed & that they might at Last on their marriage day be presented as chaste virgins to X & so were not not defiled with other with women . i.e. when the Rest of women were defiled these Kept thems. pure 4. v -------------------------

1 who they are that sing viz the 144000. ------------ i.e the X Chh. or Company of true saints. whose souls were virgins that followed -- ---------------- The numb. 144000 theref. is mystical dont signify any Certain numb. but a very Great numb tho a small numb in Comparison of those that had apostatized. It is it is a mystical numb. there is some mystery in this numb. of 144000 . we find throughout this Book that the True Chh. is alwaies Reckoned by twelves . as there were twelve Patriarch twelve sons of Jacob twelve tribes of the Chil. of Israel & twelve apostles of the Lamb Chap. 21. 12. & twelve apostles of the Lamb. ### 584. I Chron. 15:12-13

### 584.I Chron. 15'12-13(12_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

________________________________________________

I Chron. 15. 12. 13. And said unto them ye are the Chief of the Fathers of the Levites sanctify your selves both ye and your Brethren that you may bring up the ark of L. G. of Israel unto the Place that I have prepared for it . For because ye did it not at the first the Lord our God made a breach upon us . for that we sought him not after the due order.

__________________________________________________

Here I would observe 1. what that affair is about which david here gives Charge viz the affair of bringing up the ark in into the cit Zion to the Place that david had prepared for it The ark was the symbol [-] a type of X and it was the symbol of the divine Presence with that People The People Israel had no Image in their Temple to Represent their God God as the Heathens had Images in their Temples to Represent their Gods but they had an ark. or a chest overlaid with Gold wherein were the Tables of the Law Tables of the Covenant & this was the symbol of the divine Presence first in the Tabernacle & then in the temple

________________

over the ark or upon the ark there was the mercy seat which was the throne of G. [^019-note1] an where he sat & where he dwelt between the Cherubims in the Temple. Gods giving the Chil. of Israel the ark to be among them was a token that G. was among them & dwelt with that People as his People. And therefore the Presence of the ark in that Land was Esteemed the Greatest Privilege that that nation Enjoyed above [--] other nations They accounted it their Glory and so indeed it was when we Consider that that Presence of G. went with the ark. And therefore when the Philistines took the ark & carried it away out of the Land & therefore the wife of Phinehas where that heard that the ark was taken of G. was taken cried out the Glory of G. is departed from Israel because the ark of G. is taken the Glory is departed from Israel for the ark of G. is taken

________________________

[-] after the Philistines had Returned the ark it was Left a Great while at Kirjathjearim a place in the outskirts of the Land & it is said that theref it is said that the Chil. of Is. Lamented while the ark abode in Kirjathjearim that all the House of Israel Lamented after the Land. Because the time was Long even twenty years . 1 sam 7. 2. [^019-note2] They Looked upon it a sorrowful time when Israel had not G. dwelling in the midst of them but upon their utmost borders as tho he was not willing to come amongst such a sinfull People any more & was ready to depart again into & Leave em & go into another Land as he had lately done. . But after david was made King it was a Joyf. time among them for then david had Leave & direction from G. to bring up the ark of G. from Kirjathjearim into the Zion . & theref they went about this work very Joyfully. as we have an account in the 13. Chap of this Book . They were Glad bid to see the ark coming again after such Long absence but their Joy Lasted but a little while the for G. made a breach among them & turned aside to the House of Obededom the & sojourned there . But after a while david seems to have had a a second Leave & direction from G. a second time to fetch up the ark of G. into Zion & it is upon this occasion that david Gives this Charge that we have in the Text. so that the affair about which david in the Text is their treatment treatment so that his Charge here Given is about the with Relation to that which a special token of the Presence of [^019-note3] God in Israel. & was the Glory of Israel about their treatment of that which was Israels Glory special Priviledge & Glory it was concerning the Joyfull occasion of Gods coming to dwell amongst them again after Long absence

_________________________________________

2. we may observe what the Charge is that he gives which in substance is to sanctify thems. & treat the ark in a due manner

_________________________________

3. we may observe the special Reason why he was insists on such a Charge. viz what they had suffered by an undue treatment of the ark before they had not behaved them for because you did it not at the first. ---------------------- at firs They did not behave thems. as they ought to have done in bringing up the ark before they when the Levites ought to have carried they put it upon a Cart to be drawn by oxen & not G. had in the Law given direction how the ark should be carried --- but it seems they had forgotten the Law or had not been duly observant of it . & then when the oxen shook it one Uzza one that drave the Cart put forth his hand to secure it & G. break forth upon him & smote him that he died. [^019-note4]

_____________

if he had no Leave to touch the ark but only in such a manner & at such times as G. directed there was no direction in the Law about their taking hold of it on any such occasion nor would there have been any need of it if the Law had been attended & the Levites had carried the ark with Its staves instead of the oxens drawing it . But [-] by this means their Joy was at an End & G. for this Reason Refused for the Present to go up & take up his settled abode in Zion amongst his people there

______________________

but now david has Leave to Go again and bring up the ark & this is the Reason he insists in this Charge that now they should treat the ark in due order & mann in a manner agreeable to Gods word than Li that they no more suffer in Like manner for their Errour.

_______________________________________________

doc. when a People have once suffered by any Errours they have Committed in their treatment of special Priviledges and tokens of Gods Presence they should beware that they ben’t Guilty of those Errours the second time. Three Reasons may be given of the doc. [^019-note5] 1 Because what a People have suffered for their Errour 1. The st There should at all times be the utmost Care to treat the Tokens of Gods Presence in a due manner and that both upon account of the Greatness and honourableness of the being that is present & the preciousness of the Priviledge that a People have by the tokens of his Presence. The Greatness & honourableness of the being that whose Presence is manifested should Great excite to the most diligent heed how to [-] the to [-] how to behave our selves. if some Great Prince should come among us would not awaken care in us to behave our selves properly towards him should not w we be watchfull Least we should behave our selves some way indecently & offensively on such an occasion . How much more when the King of Kings comes amo manifests hims. present among a People . How carefull was Moses of his behaviour on Mt Sinai when he understood that G. was there when t when he & see the tokens of his Presence in the burning Bush

_______

& how was Jacobs attention awakened when he at Bethel when he waked out of sleep . & understood that G. was there says Gen. 28. 16. 17. says he surely G. is in that place & I Knew it not [^019-note6] & w he was afraid & said how dredfull is this place for this is none other than the House of G. this is the gate of Heaven . and so when G. came down ab on mt Sinai in the Presence of the People what care was Required of them in their behaviour how more Certain bounds & Limits set which any ever o c presumed to pass he was it it was at the [-] utmost which every one must take heed to hims. that he did not transgress Lest the L. should break forth upon him so it is said Eccles. 5. 1. 2. -------------

___________________________________________________

--------------------- which implies as much as that we ought to have the utmost care heed of [-] every step we take & all our behaviour in a place where G. is Present to be very attentive & heedfull & not foolish & Rash with our foot or our mouth when he is present that is so much above us. And then if we Consider how precious the priviledge is that a people have in the special tokens of Gods Presence it should Engaged them to treat such a Priviledge in a due manner men are wont to be very Carefull of that which they esteem as a [-] very precious it shall be safely Laid up in the Choicest place & shall be han Kept & handled with the utmost care 2. what a People have suffered for their Errour in [^019-note7] their treatmt of [-] such a Priviledge in times past ought to be taken as a warning from G. to take heed to thems. for the time to come . we should Learn Righ. by Gods Judgmts . There is a voice in Providence the Provid. of G. as well as in the O word of G. G. speaks once yea twice & mens Percieves it not but he opens mens ears to discipline by his Rod. Gods Breach

________________

that he The breach that G. upon Uzza was on purpose to teach the Chil. of Israel that Errour & it was so taken & improved.

3. If they continue in their Errour They can Expect no other than still to suffer for it . as G. has shewed his displeas. once so he will doubtless still be displeased in the commission of the same Errour. yea.

______________________________________________________

4. They if their Errour be Repeated they may well Expect Greater suf to suffer more than they did before . & that because their sin will now be added to to the same Errour they were guilty of before will be added Regardlessness obstinacy & Regardlessness of the warning G. h has given 'em & so G. will be more displeased [^019-note8] & theref Greater tokens of his displeasure may be Expected and as tis Gods design to teach men & Convince em of their Evil in his Judgmts for sin in to t his people is to teach them & Convince them of their sin if they dont hear when he calls & warns they may expect Louder warnings of the same Kinds that they may be made to hear & understand &.
