Application. I come now to make make application of this to our Case or with in this Church & Congregation [-] with Respect to our treatment of our special priviledges & tokens of Gods Presence that G. has heretofore Granted and now Grants amongst us

_______________________

In these days of the Gosp. me the Professing People of G. have no such external. tokens of token & symbol of the Pres. of G. that may Go from em & then Return to em again as the as the ark that may Go from em & come to em again as the ar the ark f went from Israel into the Land of the Philistines & sojourned in Kirjethjearim & then was brought up into the Land Jerusalem But the token of Gods Presence in his Chh. now are no spiritual. The way [^019-note9] that G. manifests hims. to be amongst a Professing People now is by the Influences & operations of his holy sp. upon them . at a time of the Pouring out of Gods Holy sp upon a Peop People G. appears more amongst them in a more Glo. manner than of old he appeared amongst the Chil. of Israel above the mercy seat over the ark.

_____________________________

we have in this Place have tried such tokens of Gods Presence amongst us in times past especially in that Remarkable season that began about seven years ago & G. is now against Granting us a measure of that same tokens of his presence. whereby we possess a much greater Priviledge than the Chil. of Israel did In having the ark among them & doubtly have as much Reaso Cause to take heed to our selves with Respect to our treatment of such a Priviledge as they had of their treatment to the had to to take care how they handled the ark. There is Great Reason to think that G. saw somethings amiss in displeasing to him in our treatment of such a Priviledge when we last Enjoyed it. you Know the awfull breach that G. made upon [^019-note10] us in that time of it towards the Close of it over one of the principal men in the Town

_____________

and the awfull breach G. [----] not Long after made in the House of Publick worship in the time of our solemn assembly. breaki putting into a terrible surprize breaking off which as suddenly brake breaking us off from the Ex Publick Exercise an accident by which many lives were Greatly Exposed. & many were sorely wounded.

_____________________

And which was is more than all G. departed from us & Left us the Joy of bringing up the ark Lasted but a little while the ark turned aside from us & did not come to take up its settled abode in our Zion. we were soon Left & sunk into a very cold dull cold dead state. which was very unexpected to our selves & others after so Great & wonderf. an appearance of G. amongst us . who would

____________________

have . . [-] It was a Joyfull time in Israel when they were bringing up the Ark from Kirjathjearim after He it had been there so Long & they had Lamented after the Land they. but how soon was their Joy at at [sic] end how unexpectedly did G. turn aside from them so who would have thought that saw how things [^019-note11] were amongst us what set a blessed Joyfull season it was in that Remarkable wonderf. season that times would be so soon & so Greatly altered. Certainly those things may be sufficient to put us upon strict Enquiring whether there was not we sought the L. after the due order whether there was not some considerable & notable Errours that we were guilty of in our treatment of so Great a Priviledge while we Enjoyed it . It Greatly concerns us to find out our former Errours & to beware of em now G. is Graciously Re Returning to us again & we are are Going about the Joyfull work of bringing up the ark the second time.. That we may so do. I shall first shew 1. shew wherein the Errour consisted that was committed in the [-] Israel in their bringing up the ark the firsed time &

______

2. shall Lead you to Enquire whether we have not been Guilty of the same Errours in the Last time of the [_ _ _] Remarkable tokens of the Pres. of G. among us & shall warn against them for the future I. I would shew wherein the [------] shew wherein the [^019-note12] Errours Consisted that was Committed in Israel in bring up the ark the first time. I It seems to have Consisted in these three things viz. Pride Irreverence & disobedience

_____

1. In their Pride. when the Children went about this Great work . they were very Joyfull it is said I Chron. 13. 8. that david & all Israel played before the Lord God with all their might and with singing & with harps & with Psalteries & with timbrels & with cymbals & with Trumpets but they seem to have gone about the work in too self-sufficient a manner not with a due sense of their own utter unworthiness exceeding sinfulness and utter unworthiness of such a Priviledge as that G. should dwell among them & and the ark of his strength should be brought to them There is this Evidence of it that [-] when they went about bringing up the ark the first time we read nothing of their offering up any sacrifices for their sins as they did the second time the second time after G. had smitten ‘em & humbled ‘em then they were sensible of their need of sacrifices for their sins & therefore went about it with sacrifices offered to G. as we Read 1 Chron. 15. 26. [^019-note13] they offered seven Bullocks & seven Rams They the first time they had forgot thems. & offered no sacrifice as not Considering how unworthy they were of such a priviledge as they hoped now to Enjoy. one would think they had reason enough to Consid their need of sacrifices but the first time Concerning Considering how G. had testified his displeas. against them in departing from them forsaking the Tabernacle of Shiloh. & deliv. the ark into Captiv. as to the Philistines as he had done and after that Remaining so long in Kirjathjearim but they did did not Reflect upon it nor consid their own unworthiness . and so it seems to have been from their Pride & selfsufficiency that when they were about such a Great work they never Consulted the Gods Law to see in what manner the ark ought to be carried they were not sensible but of their own blindness & Ignorance & the Ins they thought their own word sufficient to direct ‘em in this matter & so substituted their own Inventions in the Room of God’s Institutions . They make a put the ark into a new Cart to be drawn by oxen & that [_] a very suitable way of carrying it [^019-note14] in a proud Conceit of the sufficiency of their own word wisd. to direct them.

____________________

so it seems to have been from Pride in Uzzathat he presumed to Lay hold on the ark when he had no warrant from Gods Law so to do. he Looked upon it that he had a Great Priviledge the ar his G. had Put Great honour upon his Father Abinadabs family in the the ark had Remained there for twenty years & who should be appointed to drive the Cart in which the ark was Carried but he & Ahio & he was Lifted up with those things & his it was this that made him so forward & Presumptuous as bold so boldly to handle the ark of G.

_______________________________

2. another part of their Errour was Irreverance which was very much a fruit of the other they treated the ark that seemed sym upon which the name of the L. of Hosts was called in an an Irreverent manner . They were full of Joy & lifted up with their hearts were lifted up on the Joyfull occasion & in the midst of their Rejoicing they forgot thems. & treated & handled the ark in a bold irreverent manner. if uzza hearts had been so full [^019-note15] as it ought to have been of Reverence h would not have presumed to han [-] they would not have set oxen to draw it & if Uzzahs heart had been possessed with due Reverence he would not have presumed to touch it excepting only in as directed & Commanded by Gods Law. .

_____________________________________

3. The third part of their Errour was disobedience that they placed Relig. in external profession & shew than in obedience. There was a Great deal of profession & shew of Respect to G. among them . They gathered all Israel together from all Quarters of the Land. a mighty company of them to attend the ark. this had a Great shew of Respect

________________

& then they as the ark went along they praised G. with the its is said they played with all their might with singing with harps & with Psalteries & with timbrels & with Cymbals & with trumpets in this again they made a Great profession & shew of Respect.

_________________

& then when the oxen stmbled & shook the ark. & it seemed to be in danger of falling & Uzza put forth his hand to hold it that again had a shew of Respect . but these [^019-note16] things were only profession & shew But [-] at the same they were very negligent of to obedi to Gods Commands . when they so abounded in testimonies of Respect in profession & shew they were Greatly wanting & in obedience R for at the very time they were in direct disobedience to several some of Gods commands . as they were in carrying the ark in a cart drawn by oxen when G. had commanded that it should be carried on the shoulders of the Levites. & so Uzza brake Gods Law in Laying hold on the ark. for the ark was never to be touched but in such & such a manner & on such & such occasions as as the Law of G. particularly Prescribed.

______________________

These were the Errours that Israel were Guilty of in bringing up the ark the first time for which they suffered & for which G. for a while turned aside from them. now Let us in the

II. Place Enq. whether we han’t been guilty of the same Errours in our behaviour under that wonderf. mercy we received when gra G. gr gave such remarkable tokens of his pres. amongst us several years since. [^019-note17]

1. Let us strictly Enq. whether or no we were not too much lifted up with our priviledge was there too much of an appearance of a publick pride if I may so call it were we not lifted up with the Honour that G. had put upon us as a a people beyond most other People. were we not foolishly taken it with our Comparative honour. & pleased to see our selves lifted above others too much forgetting what a stiff necked unworthy People we are were we might well be Joyfull but did not we in the midst of our Rejoicing. & playing with harps & psalteries & cymbals forget our sins & Great unworthiness. was there not too much of an appearance of it in our talk of our what the Great work that was among us before others of other towns . was there not too much of an assuming air did it not make us something selfsufficient in our way of talking to others as tho we thought we were the People & we were confident that we our selves were Guides to the blind & a light of them that are in darkness & Instructors of the foolish & teachers of Babes Let us be strict with in Examining our selves as [^019-note18] to this matter & Let us take heed to our selves in thi to avoid all appearance of any thing of that nature for the future . it surely becomes us to considering how unworthy we are not to take the Least Glory to our selves before G. or men but Greatly to humble to our selves Let us speak humbly of our selves at before others & walk humbly before them as [-] a People that are fully sensible that no Glory belongs to them & that we in our selves are no better than the worst of our neighb. O Let us take Great heed that X have all the Glory that he alone may be exalted & no [_] that we all may be abased.

### 585. Is. 33:14(b)

### 585.Is. 33'14a(12_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

at Stockbridge \| _________________________________________\|_______________ Isai 33. 14. The sinners in Zion are af afraid fearfullness hath surprized the Hypocrites who among us shall dwell with the devouring fire who among us shall dwell with Everlasting Burnings.

____________

There are two Kinds of Persons among Gods Professing People or in the visible Zion or Chh. & the one are the truly Godly. those spoken of in the next v. to the text He wa that walketh righteously ----------------------------------- & the other are the sinners in Zion or Hypocrites. Tis to be observed that the Prophet in this Chap. speaks Interchangeably first to one & then to the other Comforting the Θ. awfully Threatening the one den & denouncing Gods wrath against them & Comforting the other with Gracious promises . as its Thus you may observe the f 5. & 6. verses there are Comfortable promises to the Godly. & then in the eight following verses are awf awful Judgemts are threatened against the sinners in Zion. & then in the two next verses again are blessed promises to the sincerely Godly. & in the former part of the 17. v. [^020-note1] & then in the Latter of the 17. & in the 18. 19. verses a again are Gracious promises to terrible threatenings to the sinners in Zion. & then in the next verses to the verses that follow again are Gracious promises to the Godly.

___________________

Our text is part of what is said in the Chap. to the sinners in Zion.

______________

in the 10. v. G. says now will I rise saith the Lord now will I be Exalted now will I lift up my self. i.e now will I in arise to Execute wrath on the ungodly . now will I be I wont Let em alone any Longer they shall see that I am not asleep & that I am not Regardless of mine own honour now will I be Exalted tho they have cast Condem Con tempt upon me yet I will vindicate the ho nour of my own majesty I will exalt my self & shew my G mine my Great ness & my awfull majesty in their destruction. now will I lift up my self now I will no longer have mine honour to be trampled in the dust by them but my Glory shall be manifested in their misery. and this I would make the subject of my pres. discourse. I will & more Particularly I would speak to these words 1. By Enquiry who are sinners in Zion 2. By shewing How fearfullness will hereafter sieze & surprize them 3. By shew the Reason of it that they will Know that they are going to be swallowed up in devouring fire Insisting on these Reasons of this fear & surprize that are mentioned in the text 4. By shewing why sinners in Zion will be Especially surprized with fear

The I. I would It may be Enquired who are the sinners in Zion. I answer that they are those that are in a natural [^020-note5] Condition amongst Gods visible People. Zion or the City of david of old was a type of the Chh. and the Chh. of G. in SS. is more frequently called by the name of Zion in SS than Perhaps by any other name some and sometimes by Zion is meant

_____________

& more Commonly by Zion is meant the Invisible true Church of X or Church the Invisible Church of true saints but at other times the visible Church Including or consisting of those that are outwardly & by profession and external priviledges the People of G. & so Zion is to be & this is Intended by Zion in this SS. text

_____________________________

The far Greater part of the Θ are Sinners Xs flock is but & Ever has been but a little flock. but the sinners of two sorts There are those that are visibly of Satans Kingdom that are without the pale of the visible Chh. such are all that dont profess the true Relig. nor do attend the external ordinances of it & there [are] the sinners in Zion [implied]

Gods in both are the objects of Gods displeasure & wrath but his wrath is more Especially manifested fested in SS. against the Latter. The sinners in Zion will have by far the lowest [check OED] place in Hell for They are Exalted nearest to heaven [^020-note6] in this Θ & they will be lowest in Hell in another Θ The same is meant in the Text by Hypocrites as sinners in Zion sinners in Zion are all Hypocrites for they make a Profession of the True Relig. they attend Gods ordinances & make a shew of being Gods worshippers but all is in Hypocrisy. The But I hasten to the

II. Thing proposed which is to shew how fearfullness will hereafter surprize the sinners in Zion 1. They will hereafter be afraid now many of them seem to have but little or no fear. they are quiet & secure nothing will awaken em the awfullest threatenings & the loudest warnings dont much move them they are not so disturbed with those things but that fear but that they can Easy Eat and drink & sleep with a quiet go about their Θly concerns with a a great degree without being distressed .

But the time will come when they will be awakened & how ever the time will come when the hardest & most stupid wretches will be awakened tho now preaching wont awaken them & tho death of their others wont awaken (later xout) make them afraid tho seeing others awakened & Converted next wont [^020-note7] much affected affect them the tho they can stand all that is to be heard & seen in a time of General outpouring of the sp. of G. yet the time will come when they will be awakened & fear will take hold of them They will be afraid of the wrath of G. they however senseless they are now they will hereafter be sensible of the awfull Greatness of G. & that it is a fearfull thing to fall into his hands.

_______________________________________________________

2. They will be surprized with fear. This seems to Imply two things viz the Greatness of their fear & the suddenness suddenness of it

1. The Greatness of their fear surprize argues an high degree of fear. . Their fear will hereafter be to the degree of Astonishment.

now some of the sinners in Zion are something afraid they have now & then some degrees of fear they are afraid tha they bent convinced that there is such a place as hell but they are afraid there is . They shall be as it were waked up out of their security secure sleep in a dismal fright They shall fore see a misery comp com an Unexpected Calamity any coming upon them in more dreadfull that they were aware of & coming at an Unexpected season:

### 586. Matt. 2:10

### 586.Matt. 2'10(12_40) edited

Jonathan Edwards

decem 1740 [preached at Springfield]

____________________________________________________

Math 2. 10. And when they saw the star they rejoiced with Exceeding Great Joy. [preached at New town]

__________________________________

Those words are a part of the story of the wise men that came from the East to see and to worship Jesus X after his Birth Tis not Known who these wise men were divi There are various opinions about it There were This is Certain that there was at that day a very Great Expectation in the Q especially of some Great Person that was to arise in Judea that was to rule over the Q which probably arose from the Jews being dispersed all over the Q as they were after the Captiv. into Babilon & so their carying the Prophecies of X w the messiah with them.

_____________

and Tis most Probable that those wise men men that came from the East were some that had recieved Instruction from the Holy writing of the Jews that had been carried into the East first to Babilon & then afterwards which m was many Hundred miles to the East of Judea & afterwards to Shushan in [Shushan- Neh. 1:1] Persia which was yet a Great deal further to the East There was daniel that Great Pro- [L. 1v.] phet advan exalted to Great dignity & there was Nehemiah & there was Elisha & mordecai & these had the Prophecy Prophecies of the old Test concerning X with them & daniel hims. who was set over the wise men of the East as their master was hims. a Great Prophet & wrote one of those Books of old Test. Prophecy & Purp Probably wrote it in Persia when he was in Great dignity there & doubt less [--] Left Instructions among the Great & wise men of that Eastern Part of the Q concerning X & probably might Leave his own prophecy & the other Prophecies of SS. concerning the messiah in their hands The word in the original that is translated wisemen is magai and Historians observe Learned men & in Learned men observe that there is to this day in Persia those Eastern Parts of the Q and particularly in Persia a sect called by this very name Magai & Mag or Magi there have & have been time out of mind that have many of the Books of the Old Testament in their hands & have had em delivered down from their forefathers for a Great many ages [L. 2r.] and it is supposed that they Recieved em from the Jews that were carried captive & particullarly from daniel.

_________________

Tis certain that those wise men or Magi that came to see & to worship f from the East to see & to worship X had some further Instruction than the & direction than they had by the Light of nature. and that two ways first they

___________________

1 first they were instructed one of the Prophecies of the old T. concerning the star that should arise out of Jacob This they probably had from Balaams Prophecy. numb. 24. 17. I shal see him but not now I shall behold him but not nigh[-] there shall come a star out of Jacob & a sceptre shall arise out of Israel that shall smite the corners of Moab & destroy all the chil. of Sheth.

_______

Balaam hims. who Prophecied thus came out of the East & probably from the same Countrey that those wise men came from & there might Leave his Prophecy

_______

& 2. They were Instruction by Immediate direction from Heaven There was extraordinary hand of G. influencing them stirring them up to come & seek X & directing them how to [L. 2v.] find him as is manifest because G. caused a miraculous star to appear for their direction This Star appeared to em in their own Countrey together with an Intimation from G. that X now appeared in Judea as appears by the 2nd v. They see this wonderfull star & they Know it was a sign that X was come & so came into Judea . . It seems this star appeared to them a while & then disappeared & they came to Jerusalem to Enquire after X & while they were diligently seeking him the star appeared to em again as in the next v. before the text. When they had heard the King they ---------------------------------------- and then come in the words of the Text & when they saw the star they Rejoiced --------- ----- four Three Things may be particularly noted. 1. whom they had been seeking they sought X they had come a very Long Journey to seek him. we read of some that came from the utmost parts of the Earth to see the wisd. of Solomon & probably those came from from as Great a distance to see & to worship [L. 3r.] X that was now born into the Q to be the saviour of the Q they had doubtless undergone a Long fatigue fatigue it was a tedious & wearisom Journey. & they had been Enquiring after X af of the Priests & scribes where they should find X & whom

__________________________________

2. We may observe how it was discovered to em where X was they saw a beauteous & bright & lovely star that was the Emblem of his Glory this arose to them to Enlighten & direct them This star Represented X who is called a star Rising out of Jacob in Balaams Prophecy & is said to be the bright & morning Star Rev. 22. 16.

_________________________

3. We may observe the Effect this discovery had upon their minds 'tis said they rejoiced with Excee it The Effect was joy in them as the star arose to their view Joy arose in their hearts .

_________

4. The degree of this Effect the Joy as exceeding Great they Rejoiced with Exceeding Great Joy. doc. when those that have been Earnestly seeking [L. 3v.] Christ come to find him have Reason to Re joice with Exceeding Great Joy.

________________________________________________

The following Reasons may be given of the doc.

_______

1. The Person that they find is Exceeding Ex cellent & Lovely. before X is found there is no thing that is truly Excellent Lovely as that is Ever found or seen. Those things that they had been conversant with before & had set their hearts upon had no true Excellency they only decieved em with a false empty shew but now they have found X they have found one that is Excell ent indeed; They see in him a real & substantial Excellency. Before that while they were m under trouble [----] they found no to stand --- many objects every one -------------- had before them only those things that were objects of terrour fear & terror such as death their own guilt the wickedness of their hearts & the wrath of G. and death and Hell by Reason of but nothing pleasant or Lovely. But they came to find X what was terrible in those objects disappears and there is a they found a Glo. object & & whose [---] such an [L. 4r.] one as they never found before for far surpsassing all things that ever they saw one of a Excellent majesty accompanied with Excellence & of perfect Purity without & brightness without any Int purer than the light of the Sun. a Infinitely farther from all deformity or defilemt than Heaven the highest Heavens thems. & this Conjoined with the sweetest Grace & Love one that Clothes hims. with mildness & meekness & Love how Refreshing & Rejoicing must this be after he they have nothing but Gods their sins staring them in the face appearing with a frightfull Countenance & the devils [------] they had before them as Gods any terrible anger & frightfull devils & deaths pale for & Ghastly countenance & fright such devils & the devouring flames of hell be fore their eyes It may be for a long time I say how exceeding Refreshing must it be to find So Lovely an an object after they have so Long had nothing but such objects before 'em

It was doubtless the more Refreshing & Joyfull to the wise men to see Xs star arise because a till till then they had h been travailing in a Great wilderness for there a vast dry dest desolate howling wilderness [L. 4v.] Lay to the East of Judea through which they travailed in seeking X where they found no pleasant objects nothing but dry sands & barren Rocks & pits & drought & fiery flyig serp serpents & wild beasts. but now they have a pleasant object a bright star arising

so sinners that seek X they seek him in a wildern. they have a wildern. to travail through before they can find him but what Exceeding Great Joy does it Cause to find so Glorious behold so Glo. an object after travailing in such a dreadfull desert. How Joyfull may it well [be] to after travailing [missing word] in the a night of Gross darkness beholding no pleasant object with having nothing but darkness before em to find behold that so bright a star star with such exceeding sweet & pleasant Refreshing beams Light . enq Enlightening and smiling upon them.

2. They find X Exceeding ready to Recieve them tho he be so Glorious & Excellent & person yet they find him ready to Recieve them such poor worthless hatefull Creatures as they are which was unexpected to em they are surprised with it they did [not] imagine that X was such a Kind of Person a Person of such Grace they heard he was an holy saviour & hated sin & they did not Imagine he would be so ready to Recieve such vile & wicked creatures as they they thought he surely woud never be willing to accept such provoking abominable sinners such Guilty wretches those that that had such abominable but Behold he is not a whits the more backward to Recieve em for them that they unexpectedly find him with open arms arms to Embrace them ready to forgive forever to forget all their sins as tho they had never been they find that [L. 5v.] he m as it were runs to meet them & makes em exceeding welcome & admits em not only to be his servants but his friends he sets em on li a st Lifts em out of the dust & sets em on his throne he makes them the Chil. of G. he smiles upon them he speaks peace to them he cheers & Refreshes their hearts he admits em unto an strict union with hims. & makes em exceeding welcome & gives the most Joyfull entertainmt & blnds hims. to them to be their friend forever. so are they surprized with their entertainmt they did not I never Imagined to find X a person of such Kind of Love & Grace as this tis beyond all Imagination or Conception.

___________________________________________

3. They that find X find that deliv. that is Exceeding Great They were in danger before in danger of the Chil. of wrath but now behold they are delivered from that wrath & how G. is become their friend. Before they were in the possession of satan [L. 6r.] a dreadfull and adversary but now they have found deliv. out of his hands X has Rescued 'em he has Conquered the strong m. armed h he has forever set em out of the devils Reach as to any Power to destroy them.

Before they were terribly afraid of death but now they have deliv. from all hurt that death could can do em they have no need now to be any at all afraid of death they may now in X set their hearts at Rest about it Heb. 2. 15. To deliv. those who through fear of death were -

They that find X find a defence that is Exceeding strong. as they are delivered so they may be sure that they shall be forever safe. he is they have Their enemies in wh that before held em in captivity was str vastly too strong for them they were in their hands as infants in the hands of Giants but now they have found one a saviour infinitely stronger than they they have found a strong Rock & an high tower w where they may dwell on high. X is between them & their Enemies like a Great Rocky steep Rocky impassable mountain t Like that mountain that divided between saul & david when saul s sought davids Life of which we read 1 Sam. 17. at the Latter End and saul went on this side of the mountain & david & his men on that side of the mountain & tis said . v. 27. theref. they [I Sam. 23:28] called called the place Selah Hammah lekoth. i.e. [L. 7r.] which signifies the Rock of divisions bec because it was the Ro mountain or Rock that divided between david & his Enemies

It seems that that mountain was a very steep high & Rocky mountain so that it was Impassable so that saul it divided between david & saul & was a defence to david so that saul could not Come at him to hurt him tho he must Just by Just by him But X is the true Selah Hammahlekoth Like a Great & high mountain of Rock that to be a wall of division between them & their Enemies Their enemies Can as soon penetrate that a Rocky mountain as they can hurt those that he are have found X.

They that have found X have a fountain that is exceeding full. full of that which they stand in need of for the supply of the wants & satisfying the cravings of their souls. full of that h a fountain of that happiness that is true happiness hap that which is exquisitely sweet. a fountain of Living waters fr from whence Ri are Conti are Rivers are Continually flowing . they that find him find Rivers [L. 7v.] of waters in a dry Place. Here they find an Ex Inexhaustible treasure. here they find Excellent food fat things full of marrow & wines on the Lees well Refined here they find that fruit that is sweet to the Taste Here they find Gold tried in the fire here is white Raiment for to Clothe them here are Crowns of Glory

Here here is enough they can desire no more the fountain is inexhaustible & never can be diminished & has not a poor begg Ragged naked hag pining begga beggar a wretched out cast a wandering Lost famishing creature Rest cause of Exceeding Great Joy when it w after it has Long wandered in the wilderness it finds such a fountain.

Be willing to Loose all other things t that you may find X . whatever has been dear or pleasant to you heretofore if it seems to stand in the way of your finding X be willing to part with it if there has be is any sin tha sin that has been especially dear to you & you are loth to part with it or if you Reform from it from for a time yet are Loth to part with it alwaies y Let that be no stick with you . be willing Ete forever to Loose that the pleasure & or profit of Your dearest Lusts. & be willing if you to part with all the Enjoymt [L. 12v.] of the Q if they st stand in the way of your finding X. be willing make all stand by & give place to this Great Business of seeking X

you if it be needf. that you shall miss of Profit or if it be needfull you shall should part with your credit or with your any manner of Qly comfort or Convenience be willing to do

forget your own Country & your Fathers House all that Ps. 45. ----- part with all yo that is dear & pleasant to you in your native Country . in i.e. in a stat your old natural state of sin & & in this your native Q that you naturally are acquainted with you & naturally Love & are acquainted with . . as Abrah. as Ruth. & as the wise men from the East they departed from their own Countrey to a Great dist. from their own Countrey to find X. next p. but one

23 be willing to Go through the difficulties of a wilderness travail to find X. so did the wise men men from the East as I told you before [L. 13r.] there was a vast wildern that Lay East of Judea. ------------------- there they met without doubt with Great difficulties a parched wildern. a Barren will ---- hideous mountains & Rocks -- & Pits & not serpents & wild beasts --- It is a Great undertaking to travail through such a wild. but tis well worth your while to find X you expect no other than to meet with d many difficulties and be prepared to Go through 'em Let it be Your Resolution that whatever opposition whatever selfdenial whatever su Labour whatever suffering is in whatever discouragemt is in the way you will press forward you will by Go through all difficulties

____________

Let there be as many mountains & Rocks & fiery flying serpents & Roaring Lions as there will yet you will ke hold on your way till you find X till you see that pleasant star Rising out of Jacob. 3. Be willing to Go a long Journey for to find [L. 13v.] X i.e. be willing to 2. If you would find X seek him as silver & search for him as for hid treasures . so we are directed to seek wisd. 2. Prov. at the beginning. what pains do men for the treasures that are hid in the Earth. how do they dig & search . what hard Labour do they undergo in digging for silver & Gold & after Earthly Jewels & if any man has Lost some precious thing. w how diligently and narrowly will they search for it . how long will he so diligently & Earnestly seek Jesus X

__________________________________________________________

4, Be willing to Go a Long Journey to find X i. e. be willing to Continue Earnestly seeking him a Long time & very Long time if you should be called to it . The wise men from the East went a Long Journey to find X they came as it were from the utmost part of the Earth . . they probably came from persia which was a Great many hundred miles so the Queen of sheba . she came from the utter- (see I Kings 10) most part of the Earth to hear the wisd. of so- [L. 14r.] lomon. & how do many men now a days Go long voyages for Earthly treasures . men how many Go to the East Indies to the opposite side of the Globe & more than half compass the Globe to Get Gold & precious stones . & will not you be willing to Go a long Journey to find & obtain Jesus X.

______________

Theref. take heed that your soul it is not with you as it was with the Chil. of Is. on their Long Journey in the wild & of whom we Read that their soul was much discouraged by the Length of the way .
